The approach predicts a point cloud in canonical space, injects predicted geometry into the diffusion process through noise inversion, and modulates the resulting noise to preserve generative flexibility for completing unobserved regions and refining visible geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation appears to be advancing 3D reconstruction from 2D images by combining the strengths of deterministic geometry prediction with the generative flexibility of diffusion models. The method aims to handle both benchmark and real-world images, suggesting broad applicability.

Method: The method consists of three key components: (1) Predicting a point cloud in canonical space from multiple input images, (2) Using noise inversion to deterministically inject predicted geometry into the diffusion process, and (3) Modulating the resulting noise to preserve generative capabilities for completing unobserved regions while refining visible geometry.

Result: The abstract mentions qualitative results showing reconstruction on benchmark images and real-world images, along with an illustration of the reconstruction-guided noise initialization and modulation process.

Conclusion: The conclusion suggests this approach successfully combines geometric precision with generative flexibility, enabling both accurate reconstruction of observed regions and plausible completion of unobserved areas in 3D reconstruction tasks.

Abstract: Qualitative results and an illustration of our core idea. Top left: reconstruction results on benchmark images. Top right: reconstruction results on real-world images. Bottom: illustration of reconstruction-guided noise initialization and modulation. Given multiple input images, we predict a point cloud in canonical space, deterministically inject the predicted geometry into the diffusion process through noise inversion, and modulate the resulting noise to preserve the generative flexibility required to complete unobserved regions and refine visible geometry.

</details>


### [25] [LAION-Mobile: Evaluating Deepfake Detectors On One Million Smartphone Photos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11134)
*Achim von Stryk,Janis Keuper*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper investigates the performance gap between deepfake detection benchmarks and real-world smartphone photography. The authors argue that modern computational photography (neural ISPs) in smartphones creates a domain shift that current deepfake detectors fail to handle. They introduce LAION-Mobile, a dataset of ~1 million smartphone images, and evaluate 12 state-of-the-art detectors, finding that none exceed AUC 0.624 on modern AI content, with 5 detectors falling below chance. The study reveals that threshold calibration on legacy GAN data leads to false alarms on real photos (17-91% FPR).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation stems from an ICML position paper observation that deepfake detectors achieve near-perfect AUC on benchmarks but may fail on modern smartphone photos due to computational photography (neural ISPs). Previous tests were only small proof-of-concept studies due to lack of large-scale datasets containing modern smartphone images.

Method: The authors created LAION-Mobile, an open dataset with ~1 million smartphone images with EXIF metadata from re-LAION-5B. They evaluated 12 state-of-the-art deepfake detectors using their original paper checkpoints on a 9,115-image evaluation sample (DIRE on 738). The evaluation tested detectors on both modern AI-generated content and real smartphone photos to assess false-alarm rates.

Result: Three key findings: (i) On modern AI content, no detector exceeds AUC 0.624, and five of twelve fall below chance. (ii) Thresholds fitted on legacy GAN data make detectors appear deployable (<11% FPR), but refitting on modern content reveals 17-91% false-alarm rates on real photos. (iii) No detector both beats chance on modern AI content and maintains a deployable real-photo false-alarm rate. The dataset covers first neural-ISP generation (2018-2020); current flagships are absent.

Conclusion: The conclusion indicates a significant gap in deepfake detection capabilities for modern computational photography. Current detectors fail to generalize to real-world smartphone images due to domain shift from neural ISPs. The authors call for new datasets and detectors that address the modern-ISP regime, as current flagship phones are essentially absent from evaluation benchmarks.

Abstract: Most Deepfake detectors report near-perfect AUC scores on their reference benchmarks. However, a recent ICML position paper argues that these evaluations collectively neglect the impact of modern smartphone photography: the widely used on-device neural image-signal processing pipelines (like multi-sensor fusion or noise and motion-blur suppression) increasingly shift the imaging paradigm from simple lens projections towards computational photography. Hence, devices actually generate, rather than record photos. This increases the risk that deepfake detectors may flag ordinary phone photos as fake. Due to the lack of large-scale datasets containing images from modern smartphones, this hypothesis has so far only been tested in small proof-of-concept studies. The aim of this paper is to close this gap. We introduce LAION-Mobile, an open dataset containing about 1 million smartphone images with EXIF metadata distilled from re-LAION-5B. Evaluating twelve state-of-the-art deepfake detectors with their original paper checkpoints on a 9,115-image evaluation sample of this pool (DIRE on 738), we report three key findings: (i) On modern AI content no detector exceeds AUC 0.624, and five of twelve fall below chance. (ii) Real-photo false-alarm rates are an artefact of threshold calibration: thresholds fitted on legacy GAN data make several detectors look deployable (less than 11 percent FPR), yet the same detectors flag 17-91 percent of real photos once the identical criterion is refit on modern content. (iii) Consequently, no detector both beats chance on modern AI content and keeps a deployable real-photo false-alarm rate. Mirroring the device mix of web collections, the corpus probes the first neural-ISP generation (2018-2020); current flagships are essentially absent, leaving the modern-ISP regime as the open gap.

</details>


### [26] [UniH$^3$: Unifying Hierarchical Homogeneity and Heterogeneity for All-in-One Medical Image Restoration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11156)
*Zhiwen Yang,Jiayin Li,Chengyu Liu,Hui Zhang,Bingzheng Wei,Yan Xu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: UniH3 is a novel all-in-one medical image restoration framework that unifies hierarchical homogeneity and heterogeneity. It addresses the limitation of existing methods that focus on inter-task heterogeneity but ignore inherent homogeneity (shared anatomical structures) in medical images. The framework introduces a Hierarchical Homogeneity Memory module to distill and retrieve homogeneity priors, and a Hierarchical Heterogeneity Balancer to mitigate task conflicts during multi-task learning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing all-in-one medical image restoration methods focus on modeling inter-task heterogeneity (different data distributions and degradation types) but neglect the inherent homogeneity present in medical images, such as widely shared anatomical structures within and across modalities. This homogeneity could be leveraged to ease model training and improve generalization.

Method: The proposed UniH3 framework consists of three key components: (1) Hierarchical Homogeneity Memory (H2M) module that progressively distills intra- and inter-task homogeneity priors from high-quality images during training and adaptively retrieves relevant priors for input-guided restoration; (2) Homogeneity-Guided Attention (HGA) mechanism that injects retrieved priors into the restoration pipeline; (3) Hierarchical Heterogeneity Balancer (H2B) that mitigates both inter- and intra-task conflicts during optimization for balanced multi-task learning.

Result: Extensive experiments on two large-scale benchmarks (MedIR-2D-500K and MedIR-3D-3K) demonstrate that UniH3 achieves state-of-the-art performance on both all-in-one and single-task medical image restoration.

Conclusion: UniH3 establishes a strong benchmark for general-purpose medical image restoration models by effectively exploiting both homogeneity (shared anatomical structures) and heterogeneity (task-specific characteristics) in medical images. The code is publicly available.

Abstract: All-in-One medical image restoration (MedIR) aims to address diverse tasks across modalities and degradation types using a single universal model. Existing methods typically prioritize modeling inter-task heterogeneity (e.g., distinct data distributions and degradation types). However, they largely neglect the inherent homogeneity present in medical images, such as widely shared anatomical structures within and across modalities, which can be leveraged to ease model training and improve generalization. To this end, we propose UniH3, a novel framework that Unifies Hierarchical Homogeneity and Heterogeneity for all-in-one medical image restoration. Specifically, to comprehensively exploit homogeneity, we introduce a Hierarchical Homogeneity Memory (H2M) module that progressively distills intra- and inter-task homogeneity priors from high-quality images during training, and adaptively retrieves the most relevant priors tailored to the input for guided restoration. These retrieved priors are then injected into the restoration pipeline via an efficient Homogeneity-Guided Attention (HGA) mechanism. Furthermore, to comprehensively address heterogeneity, we design a Hierarchical Heterogeneity Balancer (H2B) that mitigates both inter- and intra-task conflicts during optimization, facilitating balanced and effective multi-task learning. Extensive experiments on two large-scale benchmarks, MedIR-2D-500K and MedIR-3D-3K, demonstrate that UniH3 achieves state-of-the-art performance on both all-in-one and single-task medical image restoration. We hope this work establishes a strong benchmark and advances the development of general-purpose medical image restoration models. Code is available at https://github.com/Yaziwel/UniH3.

</details>


### [27] [Beyond Visual Quality: Evaluating Physical Consistency under Ego-Motion with EgoGenEval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11172)
*Yilin Long,Chenming Zhu,Zitang Gou,Jingli Lin,Tai Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: EgoGenEval is a geometry-grounded benchmark to evaluate physical consistency of visual generators under ego-motion, measuring camera motion grounding and scene state preservation. Evaluation of 16 pose-free generators reveals current models struggle with both tasks. Training studies show pairwise supervision cannot reliably improve both metrics simultaneously, suggesting a trajectory-centric paradigm is needed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current visual generators produce high-fidelity images but violate physical consistency under ego-motion, limiting use for spatial reasoning and embodied planning. Existing benchmarks focus on isolated images or single-step quality, leaving this challenge underexplored.

Method: EgoGenEval contains 1,400 cases and 2,360 target views spanning single-step and multi-step ego-motion. It measures Camera Motion Grounding (CMG) and Scene State Preservation (SSP) separately, with metrics validated against blinded human judgments. Controlled SFT studies were conducted using EgoGen-Train built from the same geometry-grounded pipeline.

Result: Evaluating 16 pose-free generators together with two pose-conditioned references reveals that current models struggle to execute camera motion while maintaining scene state, and no system performs well on both axes. Pairwise supervision does not reliably improve both CMG and SSP together - scene preservation gains only a fraction of what camera motion does even at full training pool.

Conclusion: The pairwise teacher-forced objective itself is the binding constraint, motivating a trajectory-centric paradigm that couples self-conditioned rollouts with explicit pose and visibility supervision.

Abstract: Recent visual generators produce high-fidelity images yet often violate physical consistency under ego-motion, limiting their use for spatial reasoning and embodied planning. Existing benchmarks largely focus on isolated images or single-step quality, leaving this challenge underexplored. We introduce EgoGenEval, a geometry-grounded, pose-free benchmark designed to evaluate the physical consistency of visual generators under ego-motion, and organize our study into two parts. (1) EgoGenEval contains 1,400 cases and 2,360 target views spanning single-step and multi-step ego-motion. It separately measures Camera Motion Grounding (CMG) and Scene State Preservation (SSP), with both metrics validated against blinded human judgments. Evaluating 16 pose-free generators together with two pose-conditioned references reveals that current models struggle to execute camera motion while maintaining scene state, and that no system performs well on both axes at once. (2) To examine whether benchmark-derived data can improve these capabilities, we build EgoGen-Train from the same geometry-grounded pipeline and run controlled SFT studies. These show that pairwise supervision does not reliably improve camera-motion grounding and scene-state preservation together: even at the full training pool and the longest budget, scene preservation gains a fraction of what camera motion does. This points to the pairwise teacher-forced objective itself as the binding constraint, motivating a trajectory-centric paradigm that couples self-conditioned rollouts with explicit pose and visibility supervision.

</details>


### [28] [A Multi-View and Confusion-Guided Ensemble Framework for Robust Synthetic Image Attribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11188)
*Zuomin Qu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper presents a multi-view and confusion-guided ensemble framework for synthetic image attribution (SIA), which identifies the source model of AI-generated images. The approach combines multiple architectures (FFT-ConvNeXt, DINOv2, CLIP, Xception) to capture frequency, semantic, and forensic features, with data augmentation for robustness against post-processing operations. A binary expert classifier addresses confusion between Stable Diffusion variants, and class-adaptive confidence calibration improves discrimination of challenging classes. The framework achieved 99.53% (public) and 99.20% (private) on the leaderboard.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The rapid advancement of text-to-image generation models has made synthetic image attribution increasingly important. The key challenges are: 1) Growing similarity among modern diffusion-based generators, and 2) Presence of diverse post-processing operations that degrade attribution accuracy.

Method: The proposed method integrates multiple complementary architectures: FFT-ConvNeXt (frequency domain), DINOv2 (semantic features), CLIP (visual-language), and Xception (forensic features). Extensive data augmentation simulates realistic post-processing (compression, resizing, grayscale, blur). A dedicated binary expert classifier handles confusion between Stable Diffusion 3 and Stable Diffusion 3.5 under low-confidence conditions. Class-adaptive confidence calibration improves discrimination of challenging classes like Tencent Hunyuan.

Result: The framework achieved 99.53% accuracy on the public leaderboard and 99.20% on the private leaderboard for the Synthetic Image Attribution Challenge at ICANN 2026 DLMMDD Workshop.

Conclusion: The multi-view ensemble approach effectively addresses the challenges of synthetic image attribution by combining diverse architectural perspectives and handling class confusion through specialized expert classifiers. The high accuracy (99.53%/99.20%) demonstrates the effectiveness of the proposed framework for identifying source generation models.

Abstract: Synthetic image attribution (SIA) has become increasingly important with the rapid advancement of text-to-image generation models. However, accurately identifying the source model of a generated image remains challenging due to the growing similarity among modern diffusion-based generators and the presence of diverse post-processing operations. In this report, we present a multi-view and confusion-guided ensemble framework for the Synthetic Image Attribution Challenge of the DLMMDD Workshop at ICANN 2026. Our approach integrates multiple complementary architectures, including FFT-ConvNeXt, DINOv2, CLIP, and Xception, to capture diverse attribution cues from frequency, semantic, and forensic perspectives. To improve robustness against unknown degradations and image manipulations, extensive data augmentation strategies are employed during training, simulating realistic post-processing operations such as compression, resizing, grayscale conversion, and blur. Furthermore, we analyze the confusion patterns of the ensemble model and observe severe ambiguity between Stable Diffusion 3 and Stable Diffusion 3.5. To address this issue, we introduce a dedicated binary expert classifier that is selectively activated under low-confidence conditions. We additionally apply class-adaptive confidence calibration to improve the discrimination of challenging classes such as Tencent Hunyuan. The proposed framework achieved 99.53% on the public leaderboard and 99.20% on the private leaderboard. The source code and implementation details are publicly available at https://github.com/ZOMIN28/SIA.

</details>


### [29] [CEM-TUDASR: Computationally efficient multi-modality transformer based unsupervised domain adaptive super-resolution approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11201)
*Anjali Sarvaiya,Jay Kadel,Kishor Upla,Kiran Raja*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: CEM-TUDASR is an unsupervised Transformer-based super-resolution framework for Wireless Capsule Endoscopy (WCE) image enhancement that works without paired training data. It uses a domain-adaptive degradation network to synthesize realistic WCE-like LR images from conventional endoscopy images, addressing the domain gap problem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Wireless Capsule Endoscopy enables non-invasive visualization of the GI tract, but its miniaturized optics, sensor limitations, and wireless transmission constraints result in low-resolution images with reduced visibility of diagnostically important structures like mucosal textures, vascular patterns, and anatomical details.

Method: The framework consists of: (1) A domain-adaptive degradation network that synthesizes realistic WCE-like LR images from HR conventional endoscopy images to reduce domain gap; (2) An SR generator integrating Deep Attention Blocks (DABs) and a Fusion Attention Block (FAB) to capture long-range contextual dependencies and fine local structures while preserving perceptual and structural fidelity. Training on curated Kvasir Capsule dataset with evaluation on KID and GIANA for cross-dataset generalization.

Result: CEM-TUDASR consistently outperforms existing unsupervised SR methods using no-reference quality metrics (BRISQUE, PIQE, NIQE, EndoQM). Qualitative results show improved restoration of mucosal textures, vascular patterns, and clinically relevant anatomical details. Cross-domain experiments on retinal images demonstrate framework adaptability. The model achieves high-quality reconstruction with only 2.67 million parameters and 169.94 GFLOPs.

Conclusion: CEM-TUDASR achieves a successful balance between reconstruction quality and computational efficiency, making it suitable for resource-constrained clinical and embedded endoscopic applications. The domain adaptation approach enables effective unpaired learning, and the Transformer architecture with attention mechanisms successfully captures both global context and local details.

Abstract: Wireless Capsule Endoscopy (WCE) enables non-invasive visualization of the gastrointestinal tract, but its miniaturized optics, sensor limitations, and wireless transmission constraints result in low-resolution images with reduced visibility of diagnostically important structures. This paper proposes CEM-TUDASR, a computationally efficient unsupervised Transformer-based super-resolution framework for WCE image enhancement without paired low-resolution (LR) and high-resolution (HR) training data. A domain-adaptive degradation network synthesizes realistic WCE-like LR images from HR conventional endoscopy images, reducing the domain gap and enabling effective unpaired learning. The SR generator integrates Deep Attention Blocks (DABs) and a Fusion Attention Block (FAB) to capture long-range contextual dependencies and fine local structures while preserving perceptual and structural fidelity. The model is trained on a curated dataset derived from Kvasir Capsule and evaluated on KID and GIANA for cross-dataset generalization. No-reference quality metrics, including BRISQUE, PIQE, NIQE, and the domain-specific EndoQM, show that CEM-TUDASR consistently outperforms existing unsupervised SR methods. Qualitative results further demonstrate improved restoration of mucosal textures, vascular patterns, and clinically relevant anatomical details. Cross-domain experiments on retinal images additionally demonstrate the adaptability of the framework. With only 2.67 million parameters and 169.94 GFLOPs, CEM-TUDASR achieves high-quality reconstruction while maintaining computational efficiency, making it suitable for resource-constrained clinical and embedded endoscopic applications.

</details>


### [30] [Tri-DehazeGS: Scene--Medium Decoupled Gaussian Splatting with Transmittance-Aware Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11223)
*Kui Jiang,Yang Gu,Jiacheng Liu,Shiyu Liu,Youyu Chen,Hui Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Tri-DehazeGS is a scene-medium decoupled Gaussian Splatting framework for recovering clean 3D scenes from hazy multi-view images. It represents clean scenes with Gaussian primitives and models the participating medium using an independent view-shared tri-plane field, composing hazy observations through a physical scattering model. It also introduces MD-TGC to compensate haze-suppressed gradients.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing scattering-aware Gaussian Splatting methods suffer from: (1) entangling clean scene radiance with atmospheric effects by binding medium-related variables to Gaussian primitives, and (2) under-reconstruction of distant or dense-haze areas due to weakened supervision from low-transmittance regions.

Method: Tri-DehazeGS proposes two key innovations: (1) Scene-medium decoupling - represents clean scene with Gaussian primitives and models the participating medium using an independent view-shared tri-plane field, then composes hazy observations through a physical scattering model. (2) Medium-Decoupled Transmittance Gradient Compensation (MD-TGC) - compensates haze-suppressed gradients after medium freezing without altering forward rendering.

Result: Experiments on real and synthetic haze benchmarks show that Tri-DehazeGS improves clean novel-view reconstruction.

Conclusion: The scene-medium decoupled framework with MD-TGC effectively addresses the entanglement and under-reconstruction problems in haze-aware 3D Gaussian Splatting, achieving better clean scene reconstruction from hazy inputs.

Abstract: Recovering clean 3D scenes from hazy multi-view images is challenging because haze attenuates scene radiance and introduces atmospheric scattering. Recent scattering-aware Gaussian Splatting methods introduce physical haze models into reconstruction, but they often apply degradation in image space or bind medium-related variables to Gaussian primitives, which can entangle clean scene radiance with atmospheric effects. Moreover, low-transmittance regions provide weakened supervision for Gaussian optimization, causing distant or dense-haze areas to be under-reconstructed. We argue that clean reconstruction under haze requires both scene--medium disentanglement and transmittance-aware optimization rebalancing. To this end, we propose Tri-DehazeGS, a scene--medium decoupled Gaussian Splatting framework. It represents the clean scene with Gaussian primitives, models the participating medium using an independent view-shared tri-plane field, and composes hazy observations through a physical scattering model. We further introduce Medium-Decoupled Transmittance Gradient Compensation (MD-TGC), which compensates haze-suppressed gradients after medium freezing without altering forward rendering. Experiments on real and synthetic haze benchmarks show that Tri-DehazeGS improves clean novel-view reconstruction. Code is available at https://github.com/aptx46/Tri-DehazeGS.

</details>


### [31] [When is Test-Time Adaptation Identifiable From Unlabeled Evidence?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11235)
*Kartik Jhawar,Lipo Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether the evidence used to select test-time adaptation (TTA) methods actually contains sufficient information to determine the best adaptation action. The authors show that even with a perfect selector, reliable selection is impossible when the observation channel makes different deployments appear identical despite having different TTA rankings. They formalize this in a Gaussian TTA model and validate findings on CIFAR-100-C and DomainNet-126 benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to address a fundamental limitation in TTA: while many methods exist to adapt deployed models without labels, choosing the wrong adaptation can degrade model performance. The authors seek to determine whether the evidence given to selectors inherently contains enough information to make reliable adaptation decisions.

Method: The authors analyze a finite-batch Gaussian TTA model mathematically, proving that the boundary between when "do nothing" beats mean recentering and when recentering wins scales as 1/sqrt(n). They also conduct public benchmark studies on CIFAR-100-C and DomainNet-126, testing modern TTA methods by changing only deployment structure to see if oracle actions reverse.

Result: The key finding is that reliable TTA selection is not guaranteed even with a perfect selector when the observation channel doesn't distinguish between deployments with different optimal adaptations. The boundary shrinks as 1/sqrt(n) with more samples. Empirical results show that changing deployment structure can reverse the oracle action while global order-blind evidence remains unchanged.

Conclusion: The paper provides a framework to distinguish between two failure modes: (1) a weak selector that fails to extract available information, and (2) an information channel that fundamentally cannot support the desired decision. This distinction is practical for diagnosing TTA failures in real-world deployments.

Abstract: Test-time adaptation (TTA) offers many ways to update a deployed model without labels, but choosing the wrong update can make a strong source model worse. Recent methods therefore try to predict which adaptation will work from unlabeled test data. We ask a prior question: does the evidence given to the selector contain enough information to determine the best action at all? We show that this is not guaranteed, even with a perfect selector. If an observation channel makes two deployments look the same while their TTA rankings differ, reliable selection is impossible from that channel; richer evidence can restore the decision only when it resolves the relevant ambiguity. We make this boundary exact in a finite-batch Gaussian TTA model, where doing nothing beats mean recentering for small shifts, recentering wins beyond a unique critical shift, and the boundary shrinks as $1/\sqrt n$. Public benchmark studies on CIFAR-100-C and DomainNet-126 show the same failure mode with modern TTA methods: changing only deployment structure can reverse the oracle action while global order-blind evidence remains unchanged. The result is a practical way to separate two failure modes that are usually mixed together: a weak selector versus an information channel that cannot support the desired decision in the first place.

</details>


### [32] [HALDETECT at ImageEval 2026 Shared Tasks: Answer-First Contrastive Grounding with QLoRA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11236)
*Syed Mohaiminul Hoque,Md Sakhawat Hossain*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: HALDETECT is a hallucination detection system for ImageEval 2026 Task 1b that identifies the visually grounded statement from three culturally plausible options given an image. The system uses QLoRA fine-tuning on Qwen2.5-VL-7B-Instruct and achieved 3rd place with CI 0.035. Key findings include that answer order matters more than model scale, and adaptation outperforms prompting alone.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Large multimodal models tend to hallucinate visual details, limiting their deployment for fine-grained interpretation. The task requires identifying the single visually grounded statement from three culturally plausible options, which is challenging because all options appear plausible.

Method: Framed the task as one contrastive decision, emit answer before explanation, structure reasoning around colour/texture, shape/form, and context. Best method: adapter fine-tuning with 4-bit QLoRA on Qwen2.5-VL-7B-Instruct while freezing the vision encoder.

Result: Achieved Contrastive Instability (CI) 0.035 on the 1,000-item test set, placing third of eight teams. Development experiments showed answer order can matter more than model scale, and adaptation beats prompting alone. Retrospective analysis confirmed QLoRA gain over best prompt but not the small gap between devtest-selected and best-test adapters. Data-scaling curve does not survive seed change. 35 residual errors are culturally plausible function, material, and recognition distinctions; naive adapter voting does not help.

Conclusion: The system successfully detects visual hallucinations in multimodal models through contrastive decision-making and QLoRA adaptation. Key insights: answer order effects can outweigh model scale differences; adaptation provides meaningful gains over prompting; and residual errors tend to be culturally nuanced distinctions that are difficult for models to disambiguate.

Abstract: Large multimodal models tend to hallucinate visual detail fluently, which limits their deployment for fine-grained interpretation. We present HALDETECT, our system for the English hallucination-detection track (Task 1b) of ImageEval 2026, in which a system must identify, from an image and three culturally plausible statements, the single visually grounded one. We frame the item as one contrastive decision, emit the answer before its explanation, and structure reasoning around colour/texture, shape/form, and context. Our best submitted adapter fine-tunes Qwen2.5-VL-7B-Instruct with 4-bit QLoRA while freezing the vision encoder and reaches Contrastive Instability (CI) 0.035 on the 1,000-item test set; we placed third of eight teams. Development experiments show that answer order can matter more than model scale and that adaptation beats prompting alone. Retrospective paired analysis of the released gold labels confirms the QLoRA gain over the best prompt but not the small gap between the devtest-selected and best-test adapters, and reseeding all four training sizes shows that the apparent data-scaling curve does not survive a seed change. The 35 residual errors are culturally plausible function, material, and recognition distinctions; naive adapter voting does not help.

</details>


### [33] [SCINTILLA-SNN: A Spiking Multi-Scale Selective Aggregation Network for Perineural Invasion Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11237)
*Youngung Han,Yului Jeong,Kyeonghun Kim,Dohyun Kweon,Suah Park,Hyunsu Go,Sungha Park,Anna Jung,Jinyong Jun,Yunho Choe,Yunjin Seo,Ken Ying-Kai Liao,Hyuk-Jae Lee,Nam-Joon Kim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SCINTILLA-SNN is a 3D spiking neural network for preoperative prediction of perineural invasion (PNI) in cholangiocarcinoma from MRI scans. It uses a four-stage hierarchical backbone with spiking convolutional stages and a Multi-Scale Spike Aggregation (MSSA) module to selectively aggregate sparse PNI-related evidence, achieving AUROC of 0.748 while reducing inference energy by 23.18× compared to dense computation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Preoperative prediction of PNI in CCA is clinically valuable but challenging because PNI-related MRI cues are subtle, sparse, and spatially localized around tumor boundaries. Standard 3D CNNs and transformers process volumetric data densely, which dilutes subtle PNI evidence and requires high computational cost.

Method: The proposed SCINTILLA-SNN consists of: (1) A four-stage hierarchical backbone with spiking convolutional stages and local spike window modulation stages for hierarchical volumetric representation extraction. (2) A Multi-Scale Spike Aggregation (MSSA) module that maps each spatial token to a learnable content value and modulates it with a spike-dynamics gate derived from firing rate and timestep-wise membrane-potential variability, producing a diagnostic token score for selective evidence aggregation.

Result: On a 10-year retrospective cohort of 182 CCA patients with 5-fold cross-validation, SCINTILLA-SNN achieves an AUROC of 0.748 and reduces estimated inference energy by 23.18× compared to dense MAC-only computation of the same network architecture.

Conclusion: The spiking neural network approach effectively addresses the challenge of detecting subtle, sparse PNI indicators in MRI while achieving significant computational efficiency, making it promising for clinical preoperative assessment of PNI in cholangiocarcinoma patients.

Abstract: Preoperative prediction of perineural invasion (PNI) in cholangiocarcinoma (CCA) is clinically valuable but remains challenging because PNI-related cues on magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) are subtle, sparse, and spatially localized around the tumor boundary. Standard 3D CNN and transformer architectures process volumetric data in a dense or spatially uniform manner, which can dilute subtle PNI-related evidence while requiring a large number of multiply-accumulate operations over 3D feature grids. To address these limitations, we propose SCINTILLA-SNN, a 3D spiking network composed of a four-stage hierarchical backbone and a Multi-Scale Spike Aggregation (MSSA) module for PNI prediction. The backbone extracts hierarchical volumetric representations through spiking convolutional stages and local spike window modulation stages. Given the resulting stage-wise representations, MSSA maps each spatial token to a learnable content value and modulates it with a spike-dynamics gate derived from firing rate and timestep-wise membrane-potential variability. The resulting score, referred to as the diagnostic token score, is used to selectively aggregate sparse PNI-related evidence. Experiments on a 10-year retrospective cohort of 182 CCA patients show that SCINTILLA-SNN achieves an AUROC of 0.748 under 5-fold cross-validation, while reducing the estimated inference energy by 23.18$\times$ compared with dense MAC-only computation of the same network.

</details>


### [34] [Fast and Accurate Monomodal 3D High Resolution Deep Registration of Drosophila Larval Brain Volumes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11240)
*Daniel Reisenbüchler,Yousef Sadegheih,Michael Dittrich,Pratibha Kumari,Muhammad Usman,Dorit Merhof*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A deep learning pipeline that deformably registers larval Drosophila melanogaster brains to a reference template in a single forward pass, achieving higher accuracy and 10-100x speed improvement over classical registration methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing larval brain registration pipelines are slow (minutes per brain), require per-case parameter tuning, and rely on classical optimization methods, limiting their use as routine preprocessing in neuroscience research.

Method: A trained deep registration pipeline performing deformable alignment to a reference template in a single forward pass at high spatial resolution, with preprocessing and anatomy-anchored evaluation pipeline.

Result: Outperformed 11 classical and 7 learned baselines on held-out data with different acquisition/quality strata. Improved on strongest classical baseline by 23 percentage points of anatomical landmark-local mutual information. Registers volumes 1-2 orders of magnitude faster than classical deformable pipelines and retains more accuracy as acquisition quality degrades.

Conclusion: The proposed deep larval brain registration framework is the most accurate and efficient method, suitable for routine preprocessing. The network, trained weights, and full pipeline are released as open-source.

Abstract: The larval stage of Drosophila melanogaster is a compact model system for neuroscience whose genetic toolkit allows fluorescent markers to be expressed in defined neural populations, and comparing the resulting expression patterns across animals requires every brain to be registered into a shared anatomical reference space. Existing pipelines for this task are predominantly based on classical registration methods, which perform a new optimization for each volume, often require per-case parameter tuning, and can take minutes per brain, limiting their use as a routine preprocessing step. We present a trained deep registration pipeline that deformably aligns a larval brain to a reference template in a single forward pass at high spatial resolution, on volumes that hold several times more voxels than those learned 3D registration is normally reported on, together with the preprocessing and anatomy-anchored evaluation pipeline required to apply it. Against eleven classical and seven further learned baselines on a held-out collection acquired with different acquisition and quality strata, the proposed pipeline is the most accurate, improving on the strongest classical baseline by 23 percentage points of anatomical landmark-local mutual information. It registers a volume one to two orders of magnitude faster than the classical deformable pipelines, and it retains more of its accuracy than any other method as acquisition quality degrades. The network, its trained weights and the full pipeline are released as the open-source deep larval brain registration framework: https://github.com/agentdr1/deep-larval-brain-reg

</details>


### [35] [From Evaluation to Enhancement: Benchmarking and Improving Think-with-Video Reasoning for Video Generative Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11242)
*Meng Luo,Yicheng Liu,Jiahao Wang,Yuanxing Zhang,Xin Tao,Pengfei Wan,Kun Gai,Hao Fei*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: VWG-Bench is a comprehensive benchmark for evaluating video generation models' cognitive reasoning capabilities across 9 dimensions and 38 tasks, coupled with Vid-PRE, a prompt rewriter that improves reasoning in video generators without architectural changes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing video generation benchmarks conflate visual quality with cognitive correctness, and it's unclear whether current models can genuinely "think with video" - executing symbolic rules, respecting physical laws, and pursuing intentional goals. There's a need for rigorous evaluation of reasoning capabilities beyond visual aesthetics.

Method: 1) VWG-Bench: 9 reasoning dimensions with 38 fine-grained tasks, evaluated via a three-level VLM-as-Judge protocol (video-level fluency, task-level rule adherence, sample-level goal realization). 2) Vid-PRE: A model-agnostic prompt rewriter using reinforcement learning with text-based rewards to produce concise, constraint-aware prompts.

Result: Leading models achieve strong rendering scores but consistently fail on logic-heavy and rule-constrained tasks. Vid-PRE yields substantial reasoning improvements across multiple generators without architectural modifications.

Conclusion: VWG-Bench provides a rigorous diagnostic lens for evaluating video reasoning capabilities, while Vid-PRE offers a scalable path toward improving think-with-video capabilities by offloading cognitive reasoning to a dedicated VLM through prompt enhancement.

Abstract: Video generation has advanced to produce visually compelling and temporally coherent results. Yet, whether these models can genuinely think with video--executing symbolic rules, respecting physical laws, and pursuing intentional goals--remains an open question. Existing benchmarks only partially address this, often conflating visual quality with cognitive correctness. We introduce VWG-Bench (Video World Generalist Benchmark), a comprehensive benchmark spanning 9 reasoning dimensions and 38 fine-grained tasks. To enable precise diagnosis, we design a three-level VLM-as-Judge protocol that independently assesses video-level fluency, task-level rule adherence, and sample-level goal realization. Evaluations of leading models reveal a striking gap: while models achieve strong rendering scores, they consistently fail on logic-heavy and rule-constrained tasks. To address this, we propose Vid-PRE (Video Prompt Reasoner and Enhancer), a model-agnostic prompt rewriter that offloads the cognitive burden of reasoning to a dedicated VLM. Trained via reinforcement learning with purely text-based rewards, Vid-PRE produces concise, constraint-aware prompts without the instability of video-level reward signals. Experiments show that Vid-PRE yields substantial reasoning improvements across multiple generators without architectural modifications. Together, VWG-Bench and Vid-PRE offer a rigorous diagnostic lens and a scalable path toward true think-with-video capabilities. All data and code are publicly available at https://huggingface.co/datasets/KlingTeam/VWG-Bench.

</details>


### [36] [Uncertainty DMD: Restoring Diversity in Few-Step Autoregressive Video Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11265)
*Zixuan Duan,Xunzhi Xiang,Yabo Chen,Xin Zhang,Changhan Liu,Haibin Huang,Chi Zhang,Qi Fan,Xuelong Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Few-step distillation improves efficiency of autoregressive video generation but causes diversity collapse, where different noise samples produce similar videos with weakened motion. This paper analyzes the problem in DMD-distilled AR video generators, identifies that mode-seeking bias causes similar first chunks, and deterministic AR cache propagates this collapse globally. The proposed Uncertainty DMD framework injects uncertainty at two stages: timestep perturbation for first-chunk diversity and stochastic cache-writing for later chunks. Experiments show improved diversity and motion dynamics while maintaining visual quality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The core problem is that few-step distillation (specifically DMD) improves efficiency but causes diversity collapse in autoregressive video generation - different noise samples produce nearly identical videos with weakened motion dynamics. This limits the creative and varied outputs that video generation models can produce.

Method: The method involves two key mechanisms: (1) timestep perturbation for the first chunk to increase first-chunk diversity, and (2) a stochastic cache-writing mechanism for later chunks to preserve uncertainty in autoregressive conditioning. Both mechanisms are used during both training and inference, requiring no architectural changes.

Result: Experiments show that Uncertainty DMD consistently improves diversity and motion dynamics while maintaining comparable per-sample visual quality.

Conclusion: The proposed Uncertainty DMD successfully restores stochasticity in distilled AR video generators by injecting uncertainty at two critical stages of autoregressive generation, improving diversity without sacrificing visual quality.

Abstract: Few-step distillation improves the efficiency of autoregressive (AR) video generation, but often causes diversity collapse: under the same prompt, different noise samples tend to produce highly similar videos with weakened motion dynamics. We analyze this degradation in Distribution Matching Distillation (DMD)-distilled AR video generators and find that, in the autoregressive setting, it takes the form of a structured uncertainty collapse: the mode-seeking bias of DMD maps different noise samples to nearly identical first chunks, and the deterministic AR cache then propagates this collapsed state to all subsequent chunks, turning a local loss of stochasticity at the rollout root into a global suppression of temporal variation. Based on this analysis, we propose Uncertainty DMD, a simple uncertainty-injection framework that restores stochasticity at two key stages of AR generation: a timestep perturbation for the first chunk to increase first-chunk diversity, and a stochastic cache-writing mechanism for later chunks to preserve uncertainty in autoregressive conditioning. The method requires no architectural changes and introduces only lightweight perturbation operations. The same perturbation mechanisms are used during both training and inference. Experiments show that Uncertainty DMD consistently improves diversity and motion dynamics while maintaining comparable per-sample visual quality.

</details>


### [37] [Improving Faint Object Detection for Space Situational Awareness with Variational Autoencoders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11269)
*Angela Cratere,Luca Ghilardi,Vishnu Reddy,Francesco Dell'Olio,Charalampos S. Kouzinopoulos,Roberto Furfaro*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A deep-learning pipeline (Tiny-U-Net + partial-convolution variational autoencoder astro-VAE) for enhancing faint moving object detection in optical SSA imagery through automated star removal and context-aware background reconstruction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Detecting low SNR objects in cislunar (X-GEO) environment is extremely challenging due to structured sky backgrounds, dense stellar fields, and scattered moonlight, which degrade classical detection algorithm performance.

Method: The pipeline combines: (1) Tiny-U-Net - a lightweight segmentation network to generate stellar masks; (2) astro-VAE - a partial-convolution variational autoencoder that learns statistical distribution of astronomical backgrounds and performs context-aware inpainting of masked regions. The reconstructed background maps serve as preprocessing to suppress fixed sources and background inhomogeneities before detection. Evaluation uses shift-and-stack scheme on real ground-based telescope observations targeting X-GEO region.

Result: The method reconstructs star-free backgrounds with high fidelity while preserving moving targets, significantly enhancing detectability of faint objects.

Conclusion: Provides an effective data-driven preprocessing strategy for faint moving-object detection in optical SSA scenarios.

Abstract: We present a deep-learning pipeline for enhancing the detection of faint moving objects in optical space situational awareness (SSA) imagery through automated star removal and background reconstruction. Detecting low signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) objects remains extremely challenging in optical observations, particularly in the cislunar (X-GEO) environment, where structured sky backgrounds, dense stellar fields, and scattered moonlight significantly degrade the performance of classical detection algorithms. To address this problem, the proposed pipeline combines a lightweight segmentation network (Tiny-U-Net) to generate stellar masks with a partial-convolution variational autoencoder (astro-VAE), designed to learn the statistical distribution of astronomical backgrounds and perform context-aware inpainting of masked regions. The reconstructed background maps can then be used as a preprocessing step to suppress fixed sources and background inhomogeneities prior to detection. As a proof of concept, the approach is integrated with a shift-and-stack scheme and evaluated on real ground-based telescope observations targeting the X-GEO region. Results demonstrate that the method reconstructs star-free backgrounds with high fidelity, while preserving moving targets and significantly enhancing detectability, thereby providing an effective data-driven preprocessing strategy for faint moving-object detection in optical SSA scenarios.

</details>


### [38] [Order-Aware 2.5D Multiple Instance Learning for Preoperative MRI-Based Perineural Invasion Risk Assessment in Intrahepatic Cholangiocarcinoma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11271)
*Hyunsu Go,Youngung Han,Kyeonghun Kim,Jinyong Jun,Junbeom Lee,Dohyun Kweon,Yului Jeong,Suah Park,Sungha Park,Anna Jung,Woo Kyoung Jeong,Ken Ying-Kai Liao,Hyuk-Jae Lee,Nam-Joon Kim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A novel weakly supervised deep learning framework (OAS-MIL) for predicting perineural invasion (PNI) in intrahepatic cholangiocarcinoma from preoperative T2-weighted MRI, using ordered 2.5D slab representation and multiple instance learning with combined set-attention and sequence-attention aggregation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Perineural invasion is an adverse histopathologic marker in intrahepatic cholangiocarcinoma, but can only be confirmed after surgical resection. Preoperative prediction via MRI is needed, but existing approaches lack slice- or voxel-level annotations, requiring only patient-level labels for training.

Method: OAS-MIL represents each tumor-centered MRI crop as an ordered sequence of overlapping 2.5D slabs from contiguous axial slices. A shared encoder extracts slab-level features, which are aggregated by two parallel branches: a permutation-invariant set-attention branch and a bidirectional sequence-attention branch. The model is trained using five-fold label-stratified cross-validation at the patient level.

Result: OAS-MIL achieved a mean AUROC of 0.770, outperforming the evaluated volumetric and MIL baselines.

Conclusion: Axial order provides a useful inductive bias for weakly supervised PNI prediction from MRI, demonstrating the effectiveness of incorporating spatial sequence information in multiple instance learning frameworks for medical imaging classification.

Abstract: Perineural invasion (PNI) is an adverse histopathologic marker in intrahepatic cholangiocarcinoma (ICC), but it is usually confirmed only after resection. Preoperative T2-weighted MRI may provide noninvasive imaging cues predictive of PNI, although labels are available only at the patient level without slice- or voxel-level annotations. We propose Order-Aware Slab Multiple Instance Learning (OAS-MIL), a weakly supervised framework for patient-level PNI prediction. Each tumor-centered MRI crop is represented as an ordered sequence of overlapping 2.5D slabs formed from contiguous axial slices. A shared encoder extracts slab-level features, which are aggregated by a permutation-invariant set-attention branch and a bidirectional sequence-attention branch. Using five-fold label-stratified cross-validation at the patient level, OAS-MIL achieved a mean AUROC of 0.770, outperforming the evaluated volumetric and MIL baselines. These results suggest that axial order provides a useful inductive bias for weakly supervised PNI prediction from MRI.

</details>


### [39] [SAMV-DUSt3R: Instance-Centric 3D Scene Decoupling from Sparse Multi-Views](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11279)
*Langxu Zhao,Zuan Gu,Yingdan Zhang,Pengfei Zhao,Tianhan Gao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes SAMV-DUSt3R, an end-to-end 3D reconstruction method that decouples objects from 3D scenes by integrating SAM2 2D masks into MV-DUSt3R. The method uses a Cross Flow Mask Block for instance-aware reconstruction and a Spatial RankGNN for optimal view selection, achieving 11% improvement in reconstruction precision.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The rising demand to decouple/separate individual objects from 3D scenes, enabling object-level disentanglement in 3D reconstruction.

Method: End-to-end model integrating SAM2 2D masks into MV-DUSt3R reconstruction via Cross Flow Mask Block, with Spatial RankGNN for optimal reference view selection (73.5% accuracy).

Result: 11% improvement in average reconstruction precision across various metrics compared to state-of-the-art baselines. Demonstrates strong instance-disentanglement capability.

Conclusion: The method successfully achieves object-level disentanglement without multi-stage pipelines, with clear benefits for driving, robotics, AR/VR, and heritage digitisation applications.

Abstract: With the rising demand to decouple objects from 3D scenes, we propose SAMV-DUSt3R, an end-to-end model that injects SAM2 2D masks into MV-DUSt3R reconstruction. A Cross Flow Mask Block uses these masks to steer the network toward the target instance, jointly improving shape accuracy and achieving object-level disentanglement without multi-stage pipelines. To ensure reconstruction stability, a lightweight Spatial RankGNN selects the optimal reference view with a selection accuracy of 73.5\%. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method boosts average reconstruction precision by 11\% across various metrics compared to state-of-the-art baselines. These results reveal a strong instance-disentanglement capability and clear benefits for driving, robotics, AR/VR, and heritage digitisation.

</details>


### [40] [Your Model Already Knows Don't Teach It, Learn to Ask It: Soft Prompting for Few-Shot Adaptation of Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11310)
*Gautam Rajendrakumar Gare,Siyi Li,Hewei Wang,Cesar Daniel Hernandez,Wei Zhao,Wolfgang M. Pauli,John Galeotti,Deva Ramanan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes soft prompting for few-shot object detection with vision-language models in out-of-domain settings (aerial, industrial, medical imagery). Using only 10 annotated images, optimized prompt tokens (7,168 parameters on average) match LoRA performance while training 20,000x fewer parameters and causing no forgetting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing VLM adaptation methods for few-shot object detection in out-of-domain settings include discrete prompt optimization and LoRA fine-tuning. The authors revisit a third option: soft prompting, aiming to achieve comparable performance with fewer parameters and without the catastrophic forgetting issues of LoRA.

Method: The method involves optimizing a small number of continuous prompt tokens (1-3 tokens, ~7,168 parameters) while keeping the pretrained backbone frozen. Two key design choices are identified: (1) placing prompt tokens at the cross-modal boundary between visual and text tokens, and (2) initializing prompts from the empty space token. The approach is evaluated on Roboflow20-VL (10-shot detection), NaturalBench VQA (forgetting analysis), and RoboCasa manipulation tasks.

Result: Soft prompting achieves 14.2 mAP on Roboflow20-VL (10-shot), matching the best LoRA configuration while training 20,000x fewer parameters. Unlike LoRA, soft prompting causes no forgetting: LoRA reduces NaturalBench VQA accuracy by 35% (relative), while soft prompting leaves pretrained performance unchanged. Learned tokens transfer to Qwen3.5-9B without retraining (+0.8 mAP) and can be verbalized into readable prompts competitive with prompt-search methods. On RoboCasa manipulation tasks, soft prompting matches LoRA on 2 of 3 tasks when tokens are placed at the gradient bottleneck.

Conclusion: Modern VLMs already encode much of what is needed for specialized domains; the challenge is learning how to ask. Soft prompting offers a parameter-efficient, forgetting-free adaptation method that transfers across models and can be interpreted as readable prompts, making it a promising alternative to LoRA for few-shot domain adaptation.

Abstract: We address few-shot object detection with vision-language models (VLMs) in out-of-domain settings such as aerial, industrial, and medical imagery, using only ten annotated images for supervision. Existing adaptation methods are discrete prompt optimization and LoRA fine-tuning. We revisit a third option: soft prompting, where a small number of continuous prompt tokens are optimized while the pretrained backbone remains frozen.
  We identify two key design choices. First, placing prompt tokens at the cross-modal boundary between visual and text tokens outperforms other placements (10.0 vs. 8.4 mAP). Second, initializing prompts from the empty space token outperforms semantic and random initialization.
  With these choices, one to three learned tokens (7,168 parameters on average) match the best LoRA configuration on Roboflow20-VL (14.2 mAP, 10-shot) while training over 20,000x fewer parameters. Soft prompting remains harder to optimize, exhibiting higher variance across random seeds. Unlike LoRA, however, it causes no forgetting: the LoRA rank matching our accuracy reduces NaturalBench VQA accuracy by 35% relative, rising to 56% at the largest rank, whereas soft prompting leaves pretrained performance unchanged.
  The learned tokens behave like prompts rather than weights. They transfer to a newer model without retraining (+0.8 mAP on Qwen3.5-9B) and can be verbalized into readable prompts competitive with prompt-search methods (matching DetPO and outperforming GEPA).
  The approach also extends beyond detection. On RoboCasa manipulation tasks, the frozen $π_{0.5}$ vision-language-action policy benefits from soft prompting, matching the LoRA baseline on two of three tasks when tokens are placed at the gradient bottleneck. These results suggest modern VLMs already encode much of what is needed for specialized domains; the challenge is learning how to ask.

</details>


### [41] [GRIPNet: Gaussian Radial Intensity Prior Guided Architecture for Pulmonary Nodule Detection in CT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11312)
*Haojie Yang,Ran Su*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A deep learning approach (GRIPNet) for detecting small lung nodules in CT scans, leveraging the Gaussian radial intensity pattern of nodules as a physical prior to improve detection accuracy, especially for nodules below 6mm.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Lung cancer is the leading cause of cancer deaths, and early detection via low-dose CT screening is critical. However, nodules below 6mm remain difficult to detect because existing methods treat nodules as generic objects and ignore the underlying imaging physics of their appearance.

Method: The study first discovered a regular intensity pattern in nodules: intensity peaks at the geometric center and decays radially in a Gaussian pattern (R² > 0.86 across 18,218 lesions). Based on this physics-informed prior, GRIPNet was designed with four specialized modules: (1) Pinwheel convolutions to decompose radial gradients, (2) Dual-frequency module to separate boundary detail from structural context, (3) Dilated masked attention to match decay extent, (4) Adaptive loss to reweight samples by conspicuity.

Result: GRIPNet achieved mAP@0.5 of 95.3% on KanserSet, 91.6% on LUNA16, and 97.9% on Lung-PET-CT-Dx, while maintaining real-time inference speed with improved high-IoU localization.

Conclusion: Incorporating physics-based priors (Gaussian radial intensity distribution) into neural network design significantly improves small nodule detection, demonstrating that understanding the underlying imaging physics can yield substantial gains over generic object detection approaches.

Abstract: Lung cancer causes more deaths than any other malignancy, and low-dose CT screening is the main pathway to early diagnosis. That pathway hinges on the smallest lesions, yet nodules below six millimeters remain hard to detect, because most methods treat a nodule as a generic object and ignore the imaging physics behind its appearance. We show that this appearance is highly regular. Intensity peaks at the geometric center of a nodule and decays radially in a Gaussian pattern, and a fit to 18,218 annotated lesions from three public benchmarks yields a mean radial coefficient of determination above 0.86 in every dataset and size stratum. A square convolution samples both axes uniformly and is mismatched to this radial signal, most severely for small nodules. Guided by this evidence, we propose GRIPNet (Gaussian Radial Intensity Prior Network), a detector in which every module maps to a measurable property of the intensity distribution. Pinwheel convolutions decompose radial gradients, a dual-frequency module separates boundary detail from structural context, dilated masked attention matches the decay extent, and an adaptive loss reweights samples by conspicuity. GRIPNet raises mAP@0.5 to 95.3, 91.6 and 97.9 percent on KanserSet, LUNA16 and Lung-PET-CT-Dx while sharpening high-IoU localization at real-time speed.

</details>


### [42] [Mi-Ripple: Restoring Images Degraded by Iterative AI Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11317)
*Jiayin Chen,Yicheng Xu,Muting Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Mi-Ripple is a diagnosis-guided restoration workflow that suppresses digital ripple artifacts (grid-like and granular textures) in iteratively edited images while preserving image structure. It separates periodic lattice artifacts from content-entangled textures and combines spectral notching, structure-aware smoothing, and cleaned-reference regeneration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Iterative reference-conditioned image editing introduces undesirable grid-like and granular textures, commonly called "digital ripple," which degrades image quality. Existing methods may erase legitimate details when attempting to remove these artifacts.

Method: The method comprises three key components: (1) Separating periodic lattice artifacts from content-entangled granular texture, (2) Combining selective spectral notching for low-distortion filtering when artifacts are spectrally isolated, (3) Structure-aware smoothing and cleaned-reference regeneration for visual reconstruction when filtering would erase legitimate details.

Result: Across fourteen notch-only executions, whole-image residual standard deviation ranges from 0.08 to 0.44 in CIELAB lightness units. In a paired regeneration example, reference cleaning reduces output debris density by 45%.

Conclusion: Mi-Ripple successfully links measurable artifact reduction to visibly cleaner generated images, rather than optimizing a spectral score alone, demonstrating practical effectiveness in suppressing digital ripple while preserving image structure.

Abstract: Iterative reference-conditioned image editing can introduce grid-like and granular textures, commonly described as digital ripple. We present Mi-Ripple, a diagnosis-guided restoration workflow that suppresses this digital ripple while protecting image structure. Mi-Ripple separates periodic lattice artifacts from content-entangled granular texture, then combines selective spectral notching, structure-aware smoothing, and cleaned-reference regeneration. This separation enables low-distortion filtering when artifacts are spectrally isolated and visual reconstruction when filtering would erase legitimate detail. Across fourteen notch-only executions, whole-image residual standard deviation is 0.08--0.44 in CIELAB lightness units. In a paired regeneration example, reference cleaning reduces output debris density by 45\%. Mi-Ripple links measurable artifact reduction to visibly cleaner generated images, rather than optimizing a spectral score alone.

</details>


### [43] [Predictive Multi-Landmark OCT Tracking for Increased Motion Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11330)
*Konrad Reuter,Suresh Guttikonda,Chaitali Uday Karekar,Christian Betz,Alexander Schlaefer*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper presents a predictive tracking approach for optical coherence tomography (OCT) that enables robust markerless motion tracking at high velocities up to 100 mm/s, with RMSE below 1 mm and support for up to nine consecutively tracked landmarks for 6D pose estimation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Optical coherence tomography is a promising modality for markerless motion tracking due to its high spatial resolution and inherent depth perception. However, existing OCT-based tracking approaches are limited in terms of trackable velocity, particularly when multiple landmarks are tracked sequentially for 6D pose estimation.

Method: A predictive tracking approach that propagates positional updates between multiple tracked landmarks to obtain a global pose prediction, enabling more robust tracking under high velocities.

Result: RMSEs below 1 mm for velocities up to 100 mm/s and up to nine consecutively tracked landmarks.

Conclusion: The results highlight the potential of global motion propagation and prediction for improving the robustness of OCT-based tracking.

Abstract: Optical coherence tomography is a promising modality for markerless motion tracking due to its high spatial resolution and inherent depth perception. However, existing OCT-based tracking approaches are limited in terms of trackable velocity, particularly when multiple landmarks are tracked sequentially for 6D pose estimation. In this work, we present a predictive tracking approach that propagates positional updates between multiple tracked landmarks to obtain a global pose prediction. This enables more robust tracking under high velocities. Our results demonstrate RMSEs below 1 mm for velocities up to 100 mm/s and up to nine consecutively tracked landmarks, highlighting the potential of global motion propagation and prediction for improving the robustness of OCT-based tracking.

</details>


### [44] [R4Tun: LLM-guided adaptive segmental tunnel lining segmentation in point clouds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11360)
*Xinghui Tao,Zehao Ye,Guangming Wang,Jelena Ninić,Brian Sheil*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: R4Tun is an LLM-driven adaptation framework for automated tunnel lining inspection from 3D point clouds. It extends an expert-designed pipeline (SAM4Tun) with bounded parameter tuning using structured context (memory, state, knowledge). The framework achieved significant mIoU improvements (0.18→0.43-0.48) and OA gains (0.42→0.59-0.65) across 30 tunnel subsets and 3 LLMs, with consistent cross-LLM parameter adjustment trends.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Automated inspection of segmental tunnel linings requires adaptive segmentation from 3D point clouds, but expert-tuned pipelines (like SAM4Tun) often degrade when tunnel conditions vary. There was a need for a method that can adapt pipeline parameters dynamically without manual re-tuning.

Method: R4Tun extends SAM4Tun with bounded parameter tuning informed by three context components: memory (m), state (s), and knowledge (k). The framework was evaluated on 30 Seg2Tunnel subsets (13 regular, 17 complex) using three different LLMs, totaling 270 runs (30 tunnels × 3 LLMs × 3 context settings).

Result: The full m+s+k design raised mean IoU from 0.18 to 0.43-0.48 and overall accuracy from 0.42 to 0.59-0.65 relative to the static SAM4Tun baseline. Regular (staggered) subsets achieved mIoU of 0.784-0.796 across LLMs. All three LLMs showed similar parameter-adjustment trends with overlapping 95% CIs on mean gains and consistently adjusted a shared set of critical parameters.

Conclusion: R4Tun serves as a controlled, label-free, cross-LLM adaptation mechanism in the SAM4Tun-Seg2Tunnel setting, demonstrating consistent accuracy gains. It is positioned as a mechanism contribution rather than a deployable final-inspection system, with each bounded parameter change being auditable via logged rationales.

Abstract: Automated inspection of segmental tunnel linings requires adaptive segmentation from 3D point clouds, yet expert-tuned pipelines often degrade when tunnel conditions vary. This paper presents R4Tun, a large language model (LLM)-driven adaptation framework that extends an expert-designed pipeline (SAM4Tun) with bounded parameter tuning informed by structured context: memory ($m$), state ($s$), and knowledge ($k$). Evaluated on 30 selected Seg2Tunnel subsets (13 regular, 17 complex) across three LLMs, the full $m+s+k$ design raised mean Intersection-over-Union (mIoU) from 0.18 to 0.43--0.48 and overall accuracy (OA) from 0.42 to 0.59--0.65 relative to the static SAM4Tun baseline, with the near-reference regular (staggered) subsets reaching mIoU 0.784--0.796 across LLMs. Across 270 (30 tunnels $\times$ 3 different LLMs $\times$ 3 context settings) runs, the LLMs showed similar parameter-adjustment trends (with overlapping 95\% CIs on mean gains) and consistently adjusted a shared set of critical parameters. These results support R4Tun as a controlled, label-free, cross-LLM adaptation mechanism in the tested SAM4Tun--Seg2Tunnel setting, demonstrating consistent accuracy gains; we position R4Tun as a mechanism contribution rather than a deployable final-inspection system, in which each bounded parameter change is auditable via logged rationales.

</details>


### [45] [Vision Transformer-Based Multi-Level Feature Fusion for Multi-Label Sewer Defect Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11375)
*Xu Fang,Zhuoran Wang,Qing Li,Shengyu Zhang,Guanzhi Deng,Jianbiao He,Qingquan Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper develops Sewer-Transformer-ML, a hierarchical vision Transformer with multi-level feature fusion, along with two lightweight architectures (Sewer-MobileNet-ML and Sewer-Mobile-TransNet) for automated sewer defect classification. The base model achieves state-of-the-art performance on the Sewer-ML benchmark, while the lightweight version achieves comparable accuracy with 95% parameter reduction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing deep learning methods struggle to balance classification accuracy and computational complexity in large-scale multi-label sewer defect classification scenarios. There is a need for both high-performance and resource-efficient models for practical inspection applications.

Method: The study develops three models: (1) Sewer-Transformer-ML - a hierarchical vision Transformer with multi-level feature fusion; (2) Sewer-MobileNet-ML - a lightweight CNN architecture; (3) Sewer-Mobile-TransNet - a hybrid mobile Transformer network. The approach explores different feature fusion strategies (direct concatenation vs. attention-based fusion) for Transformer and CNN features.

Result: Sewer-Transformer-ML-Base achieved F2_CIW of 65.68% and F1_Normal of 92.68%, ranking first on the public leaderboard and exceeding the second-ranked method by 7.6 percentage points. Sewer-MobileNet-ML achieved F2_CIW of 65.73% with only 17M parameters (95% parameter reduction). Sewer-Mobile-TransNet achieved 96.43% accuracy on the standard Sewer-Capsule split. Pretraining on Sewer-ML improved performance even with only 1,177 training images. Ablation showed direct concatenation works better for Transformer features, while attention-based fusion is better for multiscale CNN features.

Conclusion: The proposed models provide an effective computational basis for automated sewer inspection. The lightweight models offer practical solutions for resource-constrained scenarios while maintaining competitive accuracy. The findings support model adaptation across civil infrastructure inspection platforms.

Abstract: Automated classification of sewer defects is essential for infrastructure condition assessment and maintenance decision-making, but existing deep learning methods struggle to balance classification accuracy and computational complexity in large-scale multi-label scenarios. This study develops Sewer-Transformer-ML, a hierarchical vision Transformer with multi-level feature fusion, together with two lightweight architectures, Sewer-MobileNet-ML and Sewer-Mobile-TransNet, for resource-constrained inspection scenarios. On the Sewer-ML test set, Sewer-Transformer-ML-Base achieved an $F2_{\text{CIW}}$ of 65.68% and an $F1_{\text{Normal}}$ of 92.68%, ranking first on the public leaderboard and exceeding the second-ranked method by 7.6 percentage points in $F2_{\text{CIW}}$. Sewer-MobileNet-ML achieved an $F2_{\text{CIW}}$ of 65.73% with only 17 M parameters, representing an approximately 95% parameter reduction relative to the base model. Under the standard Sewer-Capsule data split, Sewer-Mobile-TransNet achieved 96.43% classification accuracy. When the training set was reduced to 1,177 images, pretraining on Sewer-ML consistently improved model performance. Ablation experiments further showed that direct concatenation was more effective for Transformer features, whereas attention-based fusion better supported multiscale CNN features. These findings provide a computational basis for automated sewer inspection, lightweight model design, and adaptation across civil infrastructure inspection platforms.

</details>


### [46] [Brain-PACE: A Deep Siamese MRI Framework for Modelling Longitudinal Brain Acceleration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11378)
*Samuel Maddox,Jacob Newman,Saber Sami,Michal Mackiewicz,for the Alzheimer's Disease Neuroimaging Initiative,the Australian Imaging Biomarkers,Lifestyle flagship study of ageing*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Brain-PACE is a novel deep learning method that directly estimates the pace of structural brain ageing from paired longitudinal MRI scans, identifying accelerated ageing in 42.6% of MCI patients and showing stronger associations with clinical and biological markers than indirect methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Brain age estimation is a popular research proxy for brain health, but longitudinal trajectories are poorly defined and clinical use is limited. Existing methods use repeated cross-sectional brain age estimates indirectly, which may miss important pathological changes.

Method: Brain-PACE extends the LILAC framework using a Siamese neural network architecture with spatial attention, soft label distribution learning, and Cramér distance objective. It directly estimates the pace of structural brain ageing from paired T1-weighted MRI scans.

Result: Brain-PACE identified accelerated ageing in 42.6% of MCI participants. Faster brain ageing correlated with greater functional/cognitive impairment (FAQ r=0.35, ADAS13 r=0.30, CDR-SB r=0.32) and greater regional tau burden in posterior cingulate (r=0.59), precuneus (r=0.47), and entorhinal cortex (r=0.37). These associations were stronger than indirect methods.

Conclusion: Brain-PACE is a complementary longitudinal imaging phenotype with sensitivity to clinical and biological changes in early neurodegeneration, offering improved probabilistic performance, reduced prediction bias, and measures of predictive uncertainty.

Abstract: Brain age estimation has become a popular research proxy for assessing brain health and disease, yet longitudinal trajectories of brain ageing are still poorly defined, and clinical use is limited. Building on existing Siamese longitudinal frameworks, we develop Brain-Predicted Age Acceleration (Brain-PACE) to directly estimate the pace of structural brain ageing from paired T1-weighted MRI. Brain-PACE identified accelerated ageing in $42.6$% of participants with mild cognitive impairment. Faster Brain-PACE was associated with greater functional and cognitive impairment (FAQ; $r=0.35$, ADAS13; $r=0.30$, CDR-SB; $r=0.32$) and greater regional tau burden in the posterior cingulate ($r=0.59$), precuneus ($r=0.47$), and entorhinal cortex ($r=0.37$). These associations were stronger than those observed when pace was calculated indirectly from repeated cross-sectional brain age estimates, suggesting that direct longitudinal modelling captures complementary information relevant to ongoing pathological change. Methodologically, Brain-PACE extends the LILAC framework by combining spatial attention with soft label distribution learning and a Cramér distance objective, improving probabilistic performance and reducing prediction bias while providing measures of predictive uncertainty. Together, these findings support Brain-PACE as a complementary longitudinal imaging phenotype with sensitivity to relevant clinical and biological changes in early neurodegeneration.

</details>


### [47] [DINO-Med: A Unified Patch-Based Adaptation Framework for Multi-Modal Medical Image Analysis Applied to Liver Fibrosis Staging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11380)
*Boya Wang,Ruizhe Li,Chao Chen,Xin Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper presents a unified patch-based framework to adapt natural-image foundation models (DINOv3) to multi-modal medical imaging for liver fibrosis staging, significantly outperforming handcrafted Radiomics, ResNet, and SAM-Med2D baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Addressing the significant domain gap between natural color images and multi-channel medical scans when adapting foundation models like DINOv3 to medical imaging tasks.

Method: A unified framework with training-free registration, automated localization, and mask-filtered patch extraction, using a hierarchical strategy to aggregate patch-level insights into subject-level diagnostics. Four feature representations were compared: Radiomics, ResNet, SAM-Med2D, and frozen DINOv3, all with identical MLP heads. Training focused on S1 (mild fibrosis) and S4 (cirrhosis) classes.

Result: DINOv3-based framework achieved best classification accuracy of 78.4% for S1 and 75.8% for S4, evaluated via 10 random 90%/10% train/test splits on 360 subjects from CARE 2025 Liver Track 4 cohort.

Conclusion: Pre-trained DINOv3 features significantly outperform all baselines (Radiomics, ResNet, SAM-Med2D) for liver fibrosis staging, demonstrating the potential of adapting natural-image foundation models to multi-modal medical imaging despite the domain gap.

Abstract: Adapting natural-image foundation models like DINOv3 to multi-modal medical imaging is challenging due to the significant domain gap between natural color images and multi-channel medical scans. We present a unified, patch-based framework that processes raw multimodal imaging through training-free registration, automated localization, and mask-filtered patch extraction. This architecture culminates in a hierarchical strategy that aggregates patch-level insights into subject-level diagnostics. Using liver fibrosis staging as a case study, we evaluate four patch-level feature representations: handcrafted Radiomics features, learned ResNet features, pre-trained foundation model SAM-Med2D features, and frozen DINOv3 features. To ensure a controlled comparison, all models utilize the same lightweight MLP head and are evaluated across both rigid and deformable registration settings. Our training protocol focuses on mild fibrosis (S1) and cirrhosis (S4) classes only, enabling a single classifier to address both substantial fibrosis detection and cirrhosis staging. Evaluated via 10 random train (90%)/ test (10%) splits on 360 subjects from the CARE 2025 Liver Track 4 cohort, our DINOv3-based framework significantly outperforms all baselines, achieving the best classification accuracy of 78.4% for S1 and 75.8% for S4.

</details>


### [48] [Multi-Modal Controlled Coherent Motion Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11439)
*Yifei Liu,Qiong Cao,Hongwei Yi,Huaiguang Jiang,Changxing Ding*

Main category: cs.CV
