TL;DR: MOCO is a diffusion-based framework for generating coherent 3D avatar motions from concurrent multimodal inputs (speech audio, text descriptions, trajectory) without requiring aligned training data. It uses an iterative decoupled denoising process where each modality generates independent motions that are assembled and diffused as feedback for the next iteration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing methods for multimodal motion generation face two key limitations: (1) scarcity of aligned multimodal data, and (2) reliance on sequential combination or weighted sums of individual modality motions, which often results in mismatched or unrealistic movements.

Method: MOCO proposes a decoupled diffusion-based approach: during each denoising step, the model independently generates motions for each modality from input noise, assembles body parts according to predefined spatial rules, then diffuses the combined motion to serve as input noise for the subsequent denoising step. This iterative process enables each modality to refine its contribution within the overall motion context.

Result: MOCO outperforms existing baselines on a purpose-built multimodal benchmark for multimodal motion generation.

Conclusion: The iterative decoupled denoising approach enables natural and fluid motion generation from concurrent multimodal inputs without requiring aligned training data. The method achieves coherent and synchronized behaviors by progressively harmonizing movements across modalities.

Abstract: It is natural for humans to walk and talk simultaneously. This paper tackles the challenge of replicating such natural behaviors in 3D avatar motion generation driven by concurrent multimodal inputs, such as a text description of a man walking alongside speech audio. Existing methods, constrained by the scarcity of aligned multimodal data, typically combine motions from individual modalities sequentially or through weighted sums. However, they often result in mismatched or unrealistic movements. To overcome these limitations, we propose MOCO, a novel diffusion-based framework capable of processing multiple simultaneous inputs, including speech audio, text descriptions, and trajectory data, to generate coherent and lifelike motions without requiring aligned multimodal data. Our key innovation lies in decoupling the motion generation process. During each denoising step, the diffusion model independently generates motions for each modality from the input noise and assembles the body parts according to predefined spatial rules. The resulting combined motion is then diffused and serves as the input noise for the subsequent denoising step. This iterative approach enables each modality to refine its contribution within the context of the overall motion, progressively harmonizing movements across modalities. Consequently, the generated motions become increasingly natural and fluid with each iteration, achieving coherent and synchronized behaviors. We evaluate our approach using a purpose-built multimodal benchmark. Experimental results demonstrate that MOCO outperforms existing baselines, advancing the field of multimodal motion generation for 3D avatars.

</details>


### [49] [BruNet: A Cross-Domain Transfer Framework for Bruise Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11463)
*Qiming Wang,Richard J. Motley,Ebube E. Obi,Xianfang Sun,Paul L. Rosin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: BruNet is a novel bruise segmentation framework combining a ViT-based visual encoder (DINOv3 or LingBot-Vision) with a SAM-based mask decoder, trained on HAM10000 and evaluated on a separate bruise dataset without fine-tuning. It is the first study addressing automatic bruise segmentation and outperforms CNN models, SOTA segmentation models, and various SAM-based baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Bruise segmentation is challenging due to limited data, diffuse boundaries, and highly variable appearance. Prior work focused on detection, classification, or color analysis rather than pixel-level localization. This study aims to address automatic bruise segmentation for the first time.

Method: BruNet combines a self-supervised DINOv3 or pretrained LingBot-Vision backbone as the visual encoder with a SAM-based mask decoder. The model is trained on HAM10000 skin lesion dataset and evaluated on a separate bruise dataset without additional fine-tuning.

Result: BruNet outperforms CNN-based models, state-of-the-art segmentation models, ChatGPT-4o/5-assisted SAM2 zero-shot baselines, and the medical-oriented MedSAM model, demonstrating strong cross-domain generalization to bruise segmentation.

Conclusion: This is the first study to address automatic bruise segmentation. The proposed BruNet framework achieves superior performance across diverse evaluation scenarios, showing strong generalization capability across domains from skin lesions to bruises.

Abstract: Segmenting bruises is a challenging task in medical imaging due to limited data and annotations, diffuse boundaries, and highly variable appearance. In this work, we propose BruNet, a segmentation framework that combines a ViT-based visual encoder (a self-supervised DINOv3 or a pretrained LingBot-Vision backbone) with a SAM-based mask decoder. BruNet is trained on the HAM10000 skin lesion dataset and evaluated on a separate bruise dataset without additional fine-tuning. Although a small number of prior studies have explored machine learning and computer vision for bruise analysis, existing work has primarily focused on detection, classification, or colour analysis rather than pixel-level localisation. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first study to address automatic bruise segmentation. Our results show that BruNet outperforms CNN-based models, state-of-the-art segmentation models, ChatGPT-4o/5-assisted SAM2 zero-shot baselines, and the medical-oriented MedSAM model, demonstrating strong cross-domain generalisation to bruise segmentation.

</details>


### [50] [BridgeMatch: Conditional Transport Bridges in Matching Matrix Space for 3D Deformable Registration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11472)
*Qianliang Wu,Haobo Jiang,Guangwei Gao,Shuo Chen,Jin Xie,Jian Yang,Yaqing Ding*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper presents a two-stage generative solver for non-rigid point cloud correspondence estimation. Stage I uses denoising diffusion to estimate a global matching matrix at coarse resolution, then lifts it to high resolution. Stage II refines this through a conditional transport bridge, implemented as either a deterministic CFM ODE or stochastic Brownian-bridge SDE. The method achieves state-of-the-art results on 4DMatch and 4DLoMatch, with improvements in downstream registration and cross-dataset generalization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Coarse-to-fine methods for point cloud matching reduce computational cost by selecting top-K coarse regions, but this pruning can remove weak but correct hypotheses and restrict fine matching to an incomplete search space. The authors aim to maintain the complete soft matching matrix at both coarse and high resolutions to avoid this limitation.

Method: Two-stage generative solver: (1) Stage I uses denoising diffusion to estimate a global matching matrix in compact coarse-resolution space, then lifts it to high resolution while preserving hierarchy—the lifted matrix is rank-bounded and block-constant; (2) Stage II refines the lifted matrix through a conditional transport bridge, implemented with either a deterministic endpoint-parameterized conditional Flow Matching (CFM) ODE or a stochastic Brownian-bridge SDE inspired by Schrödinger bridges. Both variants use a lifted source, time-conditioned transformer, and matching-matrix endpoint predictor.

Result: Both variants produce more accurate correspondences than compared methods on 4DMatch and 4DLoMatch, improve downstream registration (especially in low-overlap cases), and show better cross-dataset generalization on CAPE and DeepDeform without target-domain adaptation.

Conclusion: The proposed two-stage generative solver with conditional transport bridge achieves superior performance for non-rigid point cloud correspondence estimation, with significant improvements in accuracy, robustness to low overlap, and cross-dataset generalization compared to existing methods.

Abstract: Reliable non-rigid point cloud correspondences are important for deformable anatomical registration, embodied perception and manipulation, and dynamic 3D reconstruction. Coarse-to-fine methods reduce computational cost by selecting the top-\(K\) coarse regions. However, this pruning may remove weak but correct hypotheses and restrict fine matching to an incomplete search space. We present \paper, a two-stage generative solver that maintains the complete soft matching matrix at both coarse and high resolutions. Stage~I uses denoising diffusion to estimate a global matching matrix in the compact coarse-resolution space. We then lift this matrix to high resolution while preserving its hierarchy. The lifted matrix is rank-bounded and block-constant. Stage~II refines it through a conditional transport bridge. We implement the bridge with two types of dynamics: a deterministic endpoint-parameterized conditional Flow Matching (CFM) ODE and a stochastic Brownian-bridge SDE inspired by Schrödinger bridges. Both variants share the lifted source, a time-conditioned transformer, and a matching-matrix endpoint predictor. Experiments on 4DMatch and 4DLoMatch show that both variants produce more accurate correspondences than the compared methods and improve downstream registration, with larger gains in low-overlap cases. They also improve cross-dataset generalization on CAPE and DeepDeform without target-domain adaptation while using the same deformation solver.

</details>


### [51] [Pre- and Post-Treatment Brain Metastases Segmentation Using nnU-Net with Post-Processing for BraTS 2026](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11477)
*Haobin Liu,Xin Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper presents a 5-fold nnU-Net ResEnc-L ensemble for brain metastases segmentation in BraTS 2026 Task 1, achieving LW-DSC of 0.733/0.751/0.713/0.549 for ET/TC/WT/RC. The authors validate post-processing stages with out-of-fold analysis and report 13 negative results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Brain metastases exhibit high inter-lesion variability in size, enhancement pattern, and post-treatment appearance, making volumetric segmentation the central challenge. The lesion-wise Dice similarity coefficient (LW-DSC) is a detection-oriented metric that behaves differently from traditional global Dice.

Method: 5-fold nnU-Net ResEnc-L ensemble trained independently for 1,000 epochs with standard Dice + cross-entropy loss on 1,296 four-modality training cases, followed by a rule-based post-processing cascade. Validation uses five-fold out-of-fold (OOF) analysis with no model-training leakage over all training cases.

Result: Final pipeline achieves LW-DSC of 0.733 (ET), 0.751 (TC), 0.713 (WT), and 0.549 (RC) on the official validation leaderboard. OOF analysis confirms two post-processing stages as robust, per-fold-consistent improvements, while one stage only improves leaderboard but does not reproduce out-of-fold.

Conclusion: The study provides mechanistic analysis of the LW-DSC metric explaining why recall-recovering post-processing carries low risk whereas component deletion does not. Thirteen negative results are reported spanning loss engineering, alternative backbones, and inference-time settings, several contradicting widely held intuitions. Source code is publicly available.

Abstract: Brain metastases exhibit high inter-lesion variability in size, enhancement pattern, and post-treatment appearance, making volumetric segmentation of both pre- and post-treatment cases the central challenge of the BraTS 2026 Task 1 (Brain Metastases). We build a pragmatic pipeline on a 5-fold nnU-Net ResEnc-L ensemble, in which each fold is trained independently for 1,000 epochs with the standard Dice + cross-entropy loss on 1,296 four-modality training cases. This ensemble is followed by a rule-based post-processing cascade tuned for the lesion-wise Dice similarity coefficient (LW-DSC), a detection-oriented metric that behaves very differently from the traditional global Dice. The final pipeline reaches an LW-DSC of 0.733 / 0.751 / 0.713 / 0.549 on the enhancing tumour (ET), tumour core (TC), whole tumour (WT), and resection cavity (RC) sub-regions on the official validation leaderboard. Rather than trusting these leaderboard gains, we audit every post-processing stage with a five-fold out-of-fold (OOF) analysis with no model-training leakage over all 1,296 training cases, scored with the official BraTS evaluation code (BraTS_evaluation): it confirms two stages as robust, per-fold-consistent improvements while the third improves only the leaderboard and does not reproduce out-of-fold. We further provide a mechanistic analysis of the LW-DSC metric that explains why recall-recovering post-processing carries low risk whereas component deletion does not, and we report thirteen negative results spanning loss engineering, alternative backbones, and inference-time settings, several of which run counter to widely held intuitions. Source code is released under Apache-2.0 at https://github.com/hornbeamliu/brats2026-met.

</details>


### [52] [FreeFlow: A Bias-free Hierarchical Transformer for Optical Flow Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11486)
*Vladislav Bargatin,Alexander Yakovenko,Khaled Abud,Dmitriy Vatolin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: FreeFlow is a hierarchical transformer for optical flow estimation that uses a single feed-forward encoder-decoder architecture without any flow-specific components (correlation volumes, feature warping, iterative refinement). It combines three attention variants (window, shifted-window, and global attention) and achieves state-of-the-art results on Sintel, KITTI-2015, and Spring benchmarks while remaining memory efficient at 1080p inference.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional optical flow methods rely on task-specific inductive biases (correlation volumes, feature warping, iterative refinement) which constrain models to predefined heuristics, limiting expressivity and increasing pipeline complexity and computational cost. This paper aims to remove these constraints by using a pure transformer architecture.

Method: FreeFlow uses a hierarchical transformer architecture with a single feed-forward encoder-decoder. It combines three attention variants: (1) window attention for local processing, (2) shifted-window attention for cross-window information exchange, and (3) global attention operating at reduced resolution. The architecture scales naturally with model capacity from small to large variants.

Result: FreeFlow achieves state-of-the-art results on major benchmarks: Sintel (0.68/1.48 EPE on Clean/Final), KITTI-2015 (3.23 Fl-all), and Spring (3.192 1px). It remains memory efficient at 1080p inference.

Conclusion: Despite the absence of standard inductive biases commonly used in optical flow methods, FreeFlow outperforms existing approaches, demonstrating that a well-designed transformer architecture can effectively learn optical flow estimation without task-specific components. The architecture shows consistent accuracy gains from small to large variants, indicating good scalability.

Abstract: Optical flow methods typically rely on task-specific inductive biases, such as correlation volumes, feature warping, and iterative refinement, among others, to reach high accuracy. While effective, such biases constrain the model to predefined heuristics, which can limit its expressivity and lead to more complex pipelines and additional computational cost. We present FreeFlow, a hierarchical transformer built without any flow-specific components, using instead a single feed-forward encoder--decoder. FreeFlow combines three attention variants: window attention for local processing, shifted-window attention for cross-window information exchange, and a global attention operating at a reduced resolution. The resulting architecture scales naturally with model capacity, enabling a consistent accuracy gain from small to large variants. Despite the absence of standard inductive biases, FreeFlow achieves state-of-the-art results on major benchmarks, including Sintel (0.68/1.48 EPE on Clean/Final), KITTI-2015 (3.23 Fl-all), and Spring (3.192 1px), while remaining memory efficient at 1080p inference.

</details>


### [53] [Recursive Code World Models: Building Complex Worlds through Recursive Scene Programs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11499)
*Zhiqi Li,Yuxuan Liao,Bo Zhu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RCWM (Recursive Code World Models) is a framework that reconstructs complex 3D worlds as executable code programs from a single reference image, using a recursive construction solver coupled with a Recursive Scene Program (RSP) representation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Code world models represent worlds as executable programs, but existing approaches lack a method for constructing complex worlds from visual evidence. The challenge is how to build detailed, multi-scale 3D scenes from limited visual input while maintaining coherent geometry and relationships across different scales.

Method: RCWM employs a global-local-global recursion process: (1) establish the whole scene, (2) recursively reconstruct unresolved parts, and (3) revisit the whole to refine composition. Key techniques include reference-aligned views for consistent camera projection across recursion levels, parent revisitation for addressing boundary issues and spatial relations, and a vision-language coding agent that compares reference images with scene renders to guide refinement.

Result: RCWM outperforms prior code-based image-to-scene reconstruction methods on complex scenes. Ablation studies confirm the benefits of recursive construction, showing that deeper recursion calls improve finer-scale reconstruction.

Conclusion: RCWM provides a recursive construction principle for building complex executable worlds from visual evidence, enabling fine-scale structures to have their own perception-and-editing loops while preserving scene-wide geometry and relationships.

Abstract: Code world models represent worlds as executable programs, but this representation alone does not determine how to construct a complex world. We introduce Recursive Code World Models (RCWM), a framework for reconstructing complex 3D worlds in code from a single reference image. RCWM couples a Recursive Scene Program (RSP) representation with a construction solver that recursively calls itself. An RSP represents the executable world as compositional scene code, while each solver call follows the same complete process: establish the whole, recursively reconstruct unresolved parts, and revisit the whole to refine their composition. This global-local-global recursion gives fine-scale structures their own perception-and-editing loops while preserving scene-wide geometry and relationships. Reference-aligned views propagate a shared camera projection across levels, while parent revisitation addresses boundaries, spatial relations, and shared errors that emerge after local refinement. A vision-language coding agent directly compares reference images with scene renders to guide refinement, recursive descent, and return. Across complex scenes, RCWM outperforms prior code-based image-to-scene reconstruction methods. Ablation studies further support the benefits of recursive construction and suggest that deeper calls can improve finer-scale reconstruction. RCWM provides a recursive construction principle for building complex executable worlds from visual evidence.

</details>


### [54] [UBone3D: Physics-Rectified Conditional Flow Matching for Anatomical 3D Shape Completion from Ultrasound](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11506)
*Weiying Chen,Yuchong Gao,Siyuan Li,Marek Reformat,Rui Zheng,Edmond Lou*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: UBone3D is a novel framework that uses physics-rectified conditional flow matching to complete partial point clouds from 3D ultrasound scans, addressing artifacts like surface thickening, streaking, and dropouts by combining a CT-trained shape prior (BoneFM) with ultrasound formation physics (USimNet).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Three-dimensional ultrasound is a safe, radiation-free imaging modality ideal for longitudinal monitoring, but its segmentation-derived partial point clouds suffer from severe artifacts that make it challenging to recover clean and complete anatomical structures. Existing methods struggle with these artifact-laden observations.

Method: The proposed UBone3D framework uses physics-rectified conditional flow matching (CFM) for point cloud completion. It models deterministic physics artifacts (surface thickening, streaking, dropouts) via a simulated physics proxy and introduces test-time physics rectification. The completion is jointly driven by two decoupled forces: (1) anatomical plausibility from a CT-trained generative shape prior called BoneFM, and (2) physics consistency enforced by USimNet in the ultrasound formation space.

Result: Extensive experiments on both simulated and in-vivo data demonstrate significant improvements in reconstruction accuracy and anatomical fidelity over existing baseline methods.

Conclusion: UBone3D successfully addresses the challenge of reconstructing complete anatomical structures from artifact-laden 3D ultrasound point clouds by integrating physics-based constraints with deep generative modeling, offering a promising solution for clinical ultrasound imaging applications.

Abstract: Three-dimensional ultrasound (US) is a safe, radiation-free complementary modality to CT and X-rays for longitudinal monitoring, yet its segmentation-derived partial point clouds are extremely artifact-laden. Consequently, it is challenging to recover a clean and complete anatomical structure from such US point clouds. In this paper, we present UBone3D, a novel framework based on physics-rectified conditional flow matching (CFM) that performs point cloud completion directly from partial US observations. UBone3D models deterministic physics artifacts (e.g., surface thickening, streaking, dropouts) via a simulated physics proxy, and introduces test-time physics rectification to steer the shape completion. At inference, the completion is jointly steered by two decoupled forces: (1) anatomical plausibility enforced by a CT-trained generative shape prior, BoneFM, and (2) physics consistency enforced by USimNet in the ultrasound formation space. Extensive experiments on simulated and in-vivo data demonstrate significant improvements in reconstruction accuracy and anatomical fidelity over existing baselines.

</details>


### [55] [Harnessing Intrinsic Subject-Aware Attention for Controllable Multi-Subject Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11507)
*Niange Yu,Ye Tian,Biaolong Chen,Miao Lu,Aixi Zhang,Hao Jiang,Yunhai Tong,Pipei Huang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DIAL addresses two key challenges in multi-subject video generation: uncontrollable fidelity strength and semantic drift. It leverages Intrinsic Spatial Grounding Maps (ISGM) - internal attention signals in Diffusion Transformers that locate reference subjects - to guide attention during low-noise phases for controllable fidelity without retraining, and to build preference pairs for RL during high-noise phases to mitigate semantic drift.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Multi-subject video generation faces two critical challenges: (1) uncontrollable fidelity strength - users cannot precisely control how faithfully the model reproduces subject identity, and (2) potential semantic drift - the generated content may gradually deviate from the intended subject identity over time. Existing methods require retraining for fidelity control and lack effective mechanisms to maintain subject consistency.

Method: The paper makes a key discovery that certain attention blocks in Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) naturally form Intrinsic Spatial Grounding Maps (ISGM) that precisely locate reference subjects. DIAL leverages this in two phases: (1) Low-noise phase: ISGM guides attention mechanism to enable precise control over fidelity strength during inference without retraining; (2) High-noise phase: ISGM automatically builds preference pairs at no additional cost for Reinforcement Learning, which anchors the model's attention to reference subjects and mitigates semantic drift.

Result: DIAL significantly outperforms baseline models on the OpenS2V-Eval benchmark, consistently improving identity consistency and enabling controllable fidelity strength.

Conclusion: This work demonstrates that internal attention signals in DiTs can be exploited for both controllable generation and training optimization. The dual-phase approach effectively addresses both fidelity control and semantic drift without requiring model retraining for inference-time control.

Abstract: Multi-subject video generation faces two key challenges: uncontrollable fidelity strength and potential semantic drift. We address these by analyzing the internal mechanisms of Diffusion Transformers (DiTs). We found that certain attention blocks naturally form an Intrinsic Spatial Grounding Map (ISGM) that precisely locates reference subjects. Building on this insight, we propose Dual-phase Intrinsic Attention Leveraging (DIAL), a framework that uses these internal signals for both training and inference. In low-noise stages, we use ISGM to guide the attention mechanism, allowing precise control over fidelity strength during inference without retraining. In high-noise stages, we use these same maps to automatically build preference pairs at no additional cost for Reinforcement Learning (RL). This RL procedure effectively anchors the model's attention to reference subjects and mitigates semantic drift. Extensive experiments show that DIAL significantly outperforms baseline models on the OpenS2V-Eval benchmark, consistently improving identity consistency and enabling controllable fidelity strength.

</details>


### [56] [Prototype Matters: Modality-unified Prototype Self-distillation for Unsupervised Visible-infrared Person Re-identification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11514)
*Menglin Wang,Xiaojin Gong*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper addresses unsupervised visible-infrared person re-identification (VI-ReID) by proposing a prototype-based framework that jointly optimizes within-modality and cross-modality similarity relations. The method combines modality-unified prototypical contrast with prototype-guided self-distillation to achieve better modality invariance than traditional optimal transport approaches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current methods for unsupervised VI-ReID rely on optimal transport which suffers from rigid hard label assignment without considering cluster noise. Additionally, enforcing only cross-modality contrast is suboptimal because it fails to jointly optimize similarity relations within and across modalities.

Method: The proposed framework has two main components: (1) Modality-unified prototypical contrast that jointly optimizes similarity relations within and across modalities simultaneously, rather than contrasting with separate cross-modality prototypes; (2) Prototype-guided self-distillation that uses self-prototype as a steady teacher to refine instance-prototype online relations. These components are optimized in a unified framework.

Result: Extensive comparison and analysis on standard VI-ReID benchmarks validate the effectiveness of the proposed method.

Conclusion: The proposed prototype-based framework provides a simple yet effective solution for unsupervised cross-modality person re-identification, achieving better modality invariance through joint optimization of intra-modality and cross-modality similarity relations.

Abstract: Estimating reliable cross-modality association is crucial to unsupervised visible-infrared person re-ID. While optimal transport is shown to be a practical solution for cross-modality association, it suffers from the rigidness of hard label assignment without considering the impact of cluster noise. Moreover, enforcing only cross-modality contrast is also suboptimal, as it fails to jointly optimize the similarity relation within and across modality. In this paper, we propose a novel framework for cross-modality learning by well exploitation of prototypes: First, instead of contrasting with cross-modality prototypes, we show that modality-unified prototypical contrast facilitates better modality invariance by jointly and simultaneously optimizing similarity relation within and across-modality. Taking self-prototype as a steady teacher, we further refine the instance-prototype online relation through prototype-guided self-distillation. The two components are optimized in a unified framework, leading to a simple yet effective model. On standard VI-ReID benchmarks, we perform extensive comparison and analysis, validating the effectiveness of our proposed method. Code is available at: https://github.com/Terminator8758/PoSeD.

</details>


### [57] [LoopVAE: Recurrent Depth Across Scales for Visual Tokenization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11516)
*Zhiying Lu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LoopVAE introduces a recurrent visual tokenizer that reuses a single core block across multiple scales and loops, reducing parameters by 65% while maintaining high reconstruction quality (0.28 rFID, 32.54 dB PSNR) on ImageNet-256.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Hierarchical visual tokenizers typically use separate processing blocks for different spatial scales, leading to large parameter counts. This work explores parameter sharing across scales to reduce model size while preserving performance.

Method: LoopVAE reuses a scale- and loop-conditioned core block within and across scales, with resolution-changing transitions remaining independent. A four-block core executes 28 block applications per encoder/decoder. The approach uses both convolutional and Transformer operators with single- or multi-resolution latent interfaces.

Result: The 29M-parameter model achieves 0.28 rFID and 32.54 dB PSNR under ~30-epoch training, representing 65% parameter reduction versus 84M reference VAEs. A Transformer ablation shows competitive PSNR/SSIM with global sharing. Key findings: completing trained recurrence improves reconstruction; small feature updates have substantial downstream effects; truncation exposes output-range errors; fewer parameters require more arithmetic and longer runtime.

Conclusion: Recurrent depth across scales is a viable parameter-sharing design axis for visual tokenization. The tradeoff is that fewer stored weights require more computation, though LoopVAE demonstrates that significant parameter efficiency can be achieved while maintaining competitive reconstruction quality.

Abstract: Hierarchical visual tokenizers typically allocate different processing blocks to different spatial scales. We ask how much of this computation can use the same parameters. LoopVAE reuses a scale- and loop-conditioned core within and across scales, while keeping resolution-changing transitions independent. A four-block core executes 28 block applications per encoder or decoder. On ImageNet-256, the 29M-parameter convolutional model reaches 0.28 rFID and 32.54 dB PSNR under an approximately 30-epoch two-stage training budget, using approximately 65% fewer parameters than the 84M reference VAEs. A non-adversarial Transformer ablation with the same execution graph finds competitive PSNR and SSIM under global sharing, although unshared blocks improve LPIPS. Targeted loop interventions show that completing the trained recurrence improves reconstruction and that even small feature updates can have substantial downstream effects. Truncation also exposes output-range errors, distinguishing useful recurrent computation from reliable early exit. Runtime profiling reveals the execution tradeoff: fewer stored weights require more arithmetic and longer runtime in the tested configurations. With convolutional and Transformer operators and single- or multi-resolution latent interfaces, LoopVAE establishes recurrent depth across scales as a parameter-sharing design axis for visual tokenization.

</details>


### [58] [World in World: Explore the World with World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11548)
*Chenxi Song,Yanming Yang,Chi Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: World in World is a training-free inference-time interface that enables flexible control of autoregressive video world models for tasks like camera-controlled video rerendering, long-horizon revisiting, and human-motion transfer, without requiring additional training.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Autoregressive video world models enable interactive exploration but lack flexible control. Existing methods require task-specific modules or additional training. The authors aim to address viewpoint changes, synchronization, region completion, and appearance recovery without these limitations.

Method: The method converts heterogeneous control evidence (source-video observations, target-view scene projections, geometry renderings, retrieved generated states) into camera- and time-labelled clean visual states. These are read through the native self-attention of a frozen causal video model. A correspondence router combines persistent point identities with geometry to establish token correspondences. Evidence-wise attention CFG (EWA) independently regulates each auxiliary channel's contribution using attention responses from the same denoising forward pass.

Result: The method supports camera-controlled rerendering, long-horizon revisiting, and human-motion transfer using the same frozen backbone. It was evaluated on camera-controlled video rerendering under diverse viewpoint changes, assessing perceptual quality, temporal consistency, and camera-following accuracy.

Conclusion: World in World provides a training-free approach to control frozen autoregressive video models for multiple tasks through a unified interface that handles diverse control evidence via native self-attention mechanisms.

Abstract: Autoregressive video world models enable interactive, long-horizon exploration, but flexible control remains challenging. Exploring a source video from new viewpoints requires the generated rollout to remain synchronised with the recorded event, place observed content in the requested view, plausibly complete newly exposed regions, and recover previously generated appearance on revisits. Existing methods typically address these requirements through task-specific modules or additional training. We present World in World, a training-free inference-time interface that converts heterogeneous control evidence into camera- and time-labelled clean visual states, which are read through the native self attention of a frozen causal video model. The evidence comprises source-video observations, target-view scene projections, geometry renderings that guide completion of newly exposed subject regions, and retrieved generated states beyond the rolling cache. Each evidence source carries token-level support and its own availability schedule. A correspondence router combines persistent point identities with geometry to establish token correspondences, guiding supported queries towards matching source-video tokens. Evidence-wise attention CFG (EWA) then independently regulates each auxiliary channel's additional contribution using attention responses from the same denoising forward pass. The shared interface supports camera-controlled rerendering, long-horizon revisiting, and human-motion transfer with the same frozen backbone. We evaluate World in World on camera-controlled video rerendering under diverse viewpoint changes, assessing perceptual quality, temporal consistency, and camera-following accuracy.

</details>


### [59] [A Comparative Evaluation of Pre-trained Convolutional Neural Networks for Melanoma Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11550)
*Wagner Moreno Schmitz,Marco Antonio de Castro Barbosa,Thiago Magalhães Amaral,Dalcimar Casanova,Jefferson Tales Oliva*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This study evaluates pre-trained CNN architectures (ResNet50, VGG16, VGG19, MobileNet, InceptionV3) for skin lesion classification using dermatoscopic (HAM10000, ISIC 2018) and histopathological (CR-AI4SkIN) image datasets. Results show accuracy ranges of 71-84% for dermatoscopic and 72-83% for histopathological images, with ResNet50 performing best overall. Key finding: model performance varies significantly between imaging modalities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Early melanoma diagnosis is critical for patient survival, but distinguishing melanoma from other skin lesions remains clinically challenging due to visual similarity among lesion types and variability in image acquisition conditions. This motivates the exploration of AI-assisted diagnostic tools.

Method: The study evaluated five pre-trained CNN architectures (ResNet50, VGG16, VGG19, MobileNet, InceptionV3) under identical training protocols across three datasets: HAM10000 and ISIC 2018 for dermatoscopic images, and CR-AI4SkIN for histopathological images.

Result: On dermatoscopic images, accuracies ranged from 71% (InceptionV3 on ISIC 2018) to 84% (ResNet50 on HAM10000). On histopathological images, accuracies ranged from 72% (VGG19) to 83% (ResNet50) on CR-AI4SkIN. ResNet50 achieved the highest accuracy across both modalities.

Conclusion: Model performance differs significantly between dermatoscopic and histopathological imaging modalities. Architectures showing similar performance on dermatoscopic images exhibit different performance on histopathological data, highlighting the importance of modality-specific model selection for skin lesion classification tasks.

Abstract: Early diagnosis of melanoma is critical for improving patient survival rates. However, accurately distinguishing melanoma from other skin lesions remains a significant clinical challenge due to the high visual similarity among lesion types and variability in image acquisition conditions. Artificial intelligence, particularly machine learning, has emerged as a promising tool to support dermatological diagnosis by automating feature extraction from medical images. Among the available approaches, convolutional neural networks (CNNs) have demonstrated strong performance in image classification tasks, making them well-suited for analyzing both dermatoscopic and histopathological images, given their ability to capture hierarchical visual patterns relevant to lesion characterization. Nevertheless, despite numerous pre-trained CNN architectures having been proposed, selecting the most appropriate one for a given imaging modality remains an open challenge. In this study, we evaluate pre-trained convolutional neural networks (CNNs) for skin lesion classification using dermatoscopic and histopathological image datasets. Experiments were conducted on the HAM10000, ISIC 2018, and CR-AI4SkIN datasets, evaluating the ResNet50, VGG16, VGG19, MobileNet, and InceptionV3 architectures under the same training protocol. The experimental evaluation showed that the models achieved accuracies ranging from 71% (InceptionV3 on ISIC 2018) to 84% (ResNet50 on HAM10000) on dermatoscopic images. For histopathological images, accuracies ranged from 72% (VGG19) to 83% (ResNet50) on the CR-AI4SkIN dataset. The results demonstrate that model performance differs between dermatoscopic and histopathological image modalities, showing that architectures exhibiting similar performance on dermatoscopic images exhibit different performance on histopathological data.

</details>


### [60] [Learn the Solid, Not the File: Canonical Inputs for Neural Networks on CAD Boundary Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11573)
*Heinrich Jiang,Hager Yasser Mohamed,Alexander Hitt,Valeriia Lomakina,Henning Jiang,Jennifer Jang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper addresses the problem that modern CAD systems use boundary representation (B-rep), but the same 3D solid can have different B-reps due to variations in modeling approaches, geometry kernel rebuilding, or export settings. The authors demonstrate that existing B-rep encoders are not robust to these variations and propose the "canonical region graph" as a new input representation that is theoretically invariant to repartitioning and rigid motions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Modern CAD systems use B-rep as the standard format for parametric 3D models. However, the same solid can be represented by different B-reps due to various factors (different operations by engineers, geometry kernel rebuilding, export settings repartitioning faces). This variability causes existing B-rep encoders to fail catastrophically when exposed to these perturbations, limiting their practical robustness.

Method: The authors 1) tested existing B-rep encoders on standard benchmarks with perturbations, naturally occurring variations from CAD software, and a human-created dataset in FreeCAD; 2) proposed the "canonical region graph" - an input representation where nodes, features, and coordinate frame are derived from the solid itself, providing theoretical invariance guarantees on repartitioning and rigid motions.

Result: Existing B-rep encoders collapse catastrophically under B-rep perturbations. The proposed canonical region graph matches the strongest baseline on standard benchmarks while being stable under every perturbation tested.

Conclusion: The canonical region graph is a robust representation for B-rep encoding that maintains performance across different B-rep variations of the same solid, outperforming existing methods in stability while matching accuracy on standard benchmarks.

Abstract: Boundary representation (B-rep) is the standard format used by modern CAD systems for parametric 3D models. It turns out, the exact same solid can be represented by different B-reps: for example, two engineers using different operations, a geometry kernel rebuilding the file, and an export setting repartitioning faces will lead to different B-reps even though the underlying solid remains the same.
  We show that existing B-rep encoders are not robust to variation in the B-rep with the same solid on perturbations applied to standard benchmarks, naturally occurring variations inherent to CAD software, and differences in how designers model the same part via a human dataset we created in FreeCAD. The performance of popular B-rep encoders often collapses catastrophically.
  We propose the canonical region graph, an input representation whose nodes, features and coordinate frame are derived from the solid itself and show theoretical invariance guarantees on repartitioning and rigid motions. It matches the strongest baseline on standard benchmarks, and is stable under every perturbation we test.

</details>


### [61] [OmniKVQuant: KV Cache Quantization for Omni-LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11582)
*Suho Yoo,Hyunjong Ok,Jongmin Choi,Jihoo Jung,Joon Son Chung*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: OmniKVQuant is a training-free framework for 2-bit KV cache quantization in Omni-modal LLMs that addresses temporal key drift and heterogeneous value geometry issues through window-based key quantization range setting and per-modality value rotation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: As Omni-modal large language models take in audio, video and text together, their KV cache memory cost grows significantly. While KV cache quantization is the de facto approach in text-only LLMs, its application to Omni-LLMs remains unexplored. This work addresses this gap by analyzing how rotation-based KV cache quantization methods like TurboQuant behave on multimodal caches.

Method: OmniKVQuant addresses two critical issues: (i) temporal key drift by setting the key quantization range over each short window of the input stream; and (ii) heterogeneous value geometry by rotating values separately per modality. The method is training-free and includes a fused Triton decode kernel that unpacks the 2-bit cache during attention, so no dense FP16 cache is ever built.

Result: On Qwen2.5-Omni and Qwen3-Omni, OmniKVQuant enables 2-bit KV caches while substantially preserving performance across seven audio-visual benchmarks.

Conclusion: This work demonstrates that efficient KV cache quantization is viable for Omni-modal LLMs, enabling significant memory reduction (2-bit) without substantial performance degradation on multimodal benchmarks.

Abstract: As Omni-modal large language models (Omni-LLMs) take in audio, video and text together, their KV cache memory cost grows. KV cache quantization is the de facto approach in text-only LLMs, but its application to Omni-LLMs remains unexplored. In this paper, we analyze how TurboQuant, a representative rotation-based KV cache quantization method, behaves on multimodal caches and identify two critical issues: temporal key drift and heterogeneous value geometry. To address these, we propose OmniKVQuant, a training-free framework that (i) sets the key quantization range over each short window of the input stream; and (ii) rotates values separately per modality. On Qwen2.5-Omni and Qwen3-Omni, OmniKVQuant enables 2-bit KV caches while substantially preserving performance across seven audio-visual benchmarks. We further provide a fused Triton decode kernel that unpacks the 2-bit cache during attention, so no dense FP16 cache is ever built. Code: https://github.com/kaistmm/OmniKVQuant

</details>


### [62] [MMGait: Benchmarking and Unifying Gait Recognition across Heterogeneous Modalities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11601)
*Saihui Hou,Chenye Wang,Qingyuan Cai,Aoqi Li,Yongzhen Huang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper presents MMGait, a large-scale multi-sensor gait recognition benchmark capturing 5 modalities (visible, infrared, depth, LiDAR, radar), and proposes OmniGait++, a unified framework handling single-modal, cross-modal, and multi-modal recognition within a shared identity space. The method uses modality-specific front ends, a shared identity encoder, and anchor-guided fusion to handle varying modality availability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current RGB-centered gait recognition benchmarks cannot systematically examine the heterogeneous photometric, geometric, and motion cues produced by human walking. Existing approaches require separately trained experts for each modality combination, creating a scalability problem.

Method: 1) MMGait benchmark: multi-sensor dataset with 5 modalities in sequence-level correspondence, supporting single-modal, cross-modal (directed retrieval), and multi-modal recognition evaluation.

2) OmniGait++ framework:
- Modality-specific front ends to preserve modality-dependent cues
- Shared identity encoder to learn comparable identity descriptors
- Anchor-guided fusion module for aggregating modality subsets without frame-level synchronization
- Jointly trained checkpoint covering all recognition settings

Result: 1) MMGait findings: modality rankings vary with probe conditions; cross-modal alignment remains difficult; fusion provides complementary gains.

2) OmniGait++ performance: competitive with task-specific experts in many shared settings; extends to higher-cardinality fusion unavailable to fixed-pair models; demonstrates unified recognition feasibility under varying modality availability.

Conclusion: MMGait serves as a common testbed for heterogeneous gait sensing. OmniGait++ proves the feasibility of unified gait recognition across single-modal, cross-modal, and multi-modal settings using a single trained model, accommodating different modality compositions and cardinalities.

Abstract: Gait recognition is commonly studied using RGB videos or their derived silhouettes and poses. Yet human walking produces heterogeneous photometric, geometric, and motion cues that cannot be systematically examined with RGB-centered benchmarks. We present MMGait, a large-scale multi-sensor benchmark that brings visible, infrared, depth, LiDAR, and radar observations into sequence-level correspondence. It provides diverse modalities spanning appearance, contours, geometry, motion, and body structure. Under a shared impostor-augmented protocol, we evaluate single-modal recognition, cross-modal recognition via directed retrieval, and multi-modal recognition using task-specific experts. Across settings, modality rankings vary with probe conditions, cross-modal alignment remains difficult, and fusion often provides complementary gains. This analysis exposes a scalability problem: individual modalities, modality pairs, and fusion configurations are typically handled by separately trained experts. We formulate Omni-Modal Gait Recognition, which unifies single-modal, cross-modal, and multi-modal recognition within a shared identity space. OmniGait++ uses modality-specific front ends followed by a shared identity encoder to preserve modality-dependent cues while learning comparable identity descriptors. An anchor-guided fusion module aggregates modality subsets of varying size without frame-level synchronization. A jointly trained checkpoint covers all three recognition settings and accommodates modality subsets of different compositions and cardinalities. Experiments show OmniGait++ remains competitive with task-specific experts in many shared settings and extends to higher-cardinality fusion unavailable to fixed-pair models. The results establish MMGait as a common testbed for heterogeneous gait sensing and demonstrate the feasibility of unified recognition under varying modality availability.

</details>


### [63] [LangStreet: Persistent Language Fields for Anchor-Decoded Street Gaussians](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11616)
*Runyi Yang,Deheng Zhang,Xiaoye Wang,Mengjiao Ma,Lei Sun,Kanzhi Wu,Ajad Chhatkuli,Luc Van Gool,Danda Pani Paudel*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces a persistent language field called "Ours" for structured Gaussian scenes in anchor-decoded representations. The key innovation is semantic ownership, where persistent decoder slots and parent anchors own the language field rather than transient view-conditioned child Gaussians. The method uses alpha-compositing responsibilities to accumulate directional evidence at slots and represents detail through low-rank residuals in anchor-relative semantic coordinates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Language Gaussian fields assume primitive semantics remain identifiable across views, but this assumption breaks in scalable anchor-decoded representations where persistent anchors generate view-conditioned child Gaussians with varying geometry and appearance across cameras.

Method: The method uses semantic ownership where transient children route observations while persistent decoder slots and parent anchors own the language field. It employs alpha-compositing responsibilities to accumulate additive directional evidence at slots, which marginalize exactly to anchors. Weakly supported slots are completed with anchor-aligned evidence while preserving anchor direction. Slot detail is represented through low-rank residuals in anchor-relative semantic coordinates. Three variants are proposed: base (anchor features + compact slot residuals), light (anchor features only), and max (full-dimensional completed slot features).

Result: On KITTI, Ours (base) achieves 34.19 2D mIoU with 2.72 GiB effective feature footprint, nearly matching Ours (max) at 34.20 mIoU and 12.90 GiB. Similar accuracy-storage tradeoffs hold on Virtual KITTI and Waymo. The results demonstrate that language fields on view-conditioned splats require persistent semantic ownership, conserved evidence, and a hierarchy balancing stability, detail, and representation cost.

Conclusion: The proposed semantic ownership framework successfully addresses the challenge of maintaining identifiable semantics across views in anchor-decoded Gaussian representations. The low-rank residual approach (base) achieves nearly identical accuracy to full-dimensional features (max) while reducing memory footprint by ~5x, demonstrating an effective balance between stability, detail, and representation cost.

Abstract: Language Gaussian fields implicitly assume that the primitive carrying semantics remains identifiable across views. This assumption breaks in scalable anchor-decoded representations, where persistent anchors generate view-conditioned child Gaussians whose geometry and appearance vary with the camera. We introduce Ours, a persistent language field for such structured Gaussian scenes. Our key idea is semantic ownership: transient children route observations, while persistent decoder slots and their parent anchors own the language field. We use alpha-compositing responsibilities to accumulate additive directional evidence at slots; these statistics marginalize exactly to anchors. We then complete weakly supported slots with anchor-aligned evidence while preserving the anchor direction, and represent slot detail through low-rank residuals in anchor-relative semantic coordinates. Our primary model, Ours (base), stores anchor features together with compact slot residuals. Ours (light) retains only anchor features, whereas Ours (max) stores the full-dimensional completed slot features explicitly. Without scene-specific semantic optimization, Ours (base) nearly matches Ours (max) across KITTI, Virtual KITTI, and Waymo. On KITTI, it achieves 34.19 2D mIoU with a 2.72 GiB effective feature footprint, compared with 34.20 mIoU and 12.90 GiB for Ours (max). The same accuracy-storage trend holds on Virtual KITTI and Waymo. These results show that language fields on view-conditioned splats require persistent semantic ownership, conserved evidence, and a hierarchy that balances stability, detail, and representation cost. Our code, checkpoints, and benchmark suite will be publicly available.

</details>


### [64] [Vidu S2: Real-Time Interactive, Editable, and Spatial Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11638)
*Jintao Zhang,Kai Jiang,Jintao Chen,Xu Wang,Deyuan Liu,Jungang Li,Dechuang Chen,Ming Lin,Jingjiang Zhou,Haopeng Jin,Qi Jia,Xiaohang Wang,Yaole Wang,Zhanqiang Zhang,Ran Li,Zhengkun Huang,Shuyue Xiong,Yuji Wang,Zikun Dai,Hui He,Yang Luo,Mang Ning,Weiqi Feng,Chengyang Ye,Xinyue Lin,Min Zhao,Hongzhou Zhu,Hengkai Tan,Zeyuan Wang,Chendong Xiang,Kaiwen Zheng,Zhijie Deng,Fan Bao,Jianfei Chen,Jun Zhu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Vidu S2 is a real-time video generation system featuring two components: Vidu S2-Avatar (interactive digital character model) and Vidu S2-Editing (real-time video editing model). It supports 720p real-time generation with dynamic references and various editing capabilities including style rendering, clothing/character/background replacement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper aims to advance real-time video generation capabilities beyond the previous Vidu S1 system, addressing the need for interactive digital characters and real-time video editing functionality.

Method: Vidu S2 includes: (1) Vidu S2-Avatar - supports real-time 720p video generation with dynamic references that can be updated anytime, and stronger instruction following (e.g., dancing); (2) Vidu S2-Editing - enables real-time editing of video streams including style rendering, clothing replacement, character replacement, and background replacement. The system also explores real-time spatial video generation.

Result: Experiments show that Vidu S2 outperforms all baselines. A playable online demo is available.

Conclusion: Vidu S2 represents an advancement in real-time video generation technology, successfully combining avatar interaction and video editing capabilities in a real-time system.

Abstract: We present Vidu S2, which comprises Vidu S2-Avatar, a real-time interactive digital-character model, and Vidu S2-Editing, a real-time video editing model. Moreover, we explore the feasibility of real-time spatial video generation for both Vidu S2-Avatar and Vidu S2-Editing. Compared with Vidu S1, Vidu S2-Avatar supports real-time 720p video generation, generation with dynamic references that can be updated at any moment, and stronger instruction following, such as dancing. Vidu S2-Editing supports editing a video stream in real time, including style rendering, clothing replacement, character replacement, and background replacement. Experiments show that Vidu S2 outperforms all baselines. A playable online demo is available at https://vidu.com/vidu-stream.

</details>


### [65] [Self-Supervised Cardiac Phase Detection via Single-Parameter Latent Orbits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11650)
*John Bonnici,Matthew Baugh,Aleksandra Kulbaka,Sarah Cechnicka,Bernhard Kainz,Alberto Gomez*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces a self-supervised deep learning method for identifying end-diastole (ED) and end-systole (ES) frames in echocardiography. The method constrains latent motion to a single-parameter "latent orbit" with sinusoidal nonlinearity, enabling direct cardiac phase identification without strict periodic assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Manual selection of ED and ES frames in echocardiography is subjective and introduces clinically significant inter-operator variability. Existing self-supervised methods either impose restrictive periodic assumptions or require post-hoc geometric processing to recover cardiac phase from unconstrained motion subspaces.

Method: The approach uses a physiological inductive bias: constraining latent motion to a single-parameter latent orbit (global linear trajectory in latent space) indexed by a bounded scalar phase variable. This phase variable is mapped through a sinusoidal nonlinearity to yield oscillatory motion with consistent temporal ordering. The model is trained on EchoNet-Dynamic without annotations.

Result: The single-parameter cardiac phase model significantly improves upon previous state-of-the-art in ED localization and matches it in ES localization, while using a more constrained representation and fewer training epochs.

Conclusion: A principled physiological inductive bias (single-parameter latent orbit) can match or exceed the performance of more complex representations, demonstrating that interpretable cardiac phase modeling does not require sacrificing accuracy.

Abstract: Accurate identification of end-diastole (ED) and end-systole (ES) in echocardiography underpins the quantification of ventricular function, yet manual selection of these key frames is subjective and introduces clinically significant inter-operator variability. Recent self-supervised methods either prescribe strict periodic trajectories or learn an unconstrained low-dimensional motion subspace from reconstruction or registration objectives. The former offers interpretability but imposes restrictive assumptions on temporal progression, whereas the latter leaves cardiac phase implicit and ED/ES must be recovered through post-hoc geometric processing of the learned trajectory. We translate the physiological observation that cardiac phase is a one-dimensional signal into a prior by constraining the latent motion component to a single-parameter latent orbit, i.e., a global linear trajectory in latent space indexed by a bounded scalar phase variable. Mapping this variable through a sinusoidal nonlinearity yields an oscillatory motion signal with consistent temporal ordering, enabling direct identification of ED and ES from the learned phase signal. This inductive bias allows the model to capture an interpretable representation of the cardiac cycle, while maintaining flexibility to capture irregular heartbeats. Trained on EchoNet-Dynamic without annotations, our minimal single-parameter cardiac phase model learns an effective latent orbit, significantly improves upon the previous state of the art in ED localisation and matches it in ES localisation while using a more constrained representation and fewer training epochs. This demonstrates that a principled physiological inductive bias can match or exceed the performance of more complex representations. Code is available at: https://github.com/BonniciJ/OrbitalEcho/

</details>


### [66] [Multimodal Taxonomic Conditioning for Generative Plankton Imagery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11673)
*Daniela Ivanova,Ozgu Goksu,Nicolas Pugeault*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper addresses the challenge of severely long-tailed datasets in automated plankton imaging, where rare taxa have insufficient images for classifier training/evaluation. The authors propose generating synthetic plankton imagery using a CLIP encoder adapted with a ranked contrastive objective for deep, ragged taxonomies, which then conditions a parameter-efficient diffusion transformer.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Automated plankton imaging produces long-tailed datasets where rare taxa of greatest ecological interest have too few images to train or evaluate classifiers reliably. This creates a significant problem for ecological research and monitoring.

Method: The method involves: 1) Adapting a CLIP encoder on a large plankton corpus using a ranked contrastive objective extended to deep, ragged taxonomies; 2) Freezing the encoder to condition a parameter-efficient diffusion transformer; 3) Generating synthetic plankton imagery conditioned on taxonomy.

Result: The paper evaluates synthetic sample quality on two dimensions: distributional fidelity and downstream classifier utility.

Conclusion: The approach provides a solution for generating synthetic plankton imagery to address the long-tailed distribution problem, enabling better training and evaluation of classifiers for rare taxa.

Abstract: Automated plankton imaging produces severely long-tailed datasets, where the rare taxa of greatest ecological interest have too few images to train or evaluate classifiers reliably. We generate synthetic plankton imagery conditioned on taxonomy: a CLIP encoder is adapted on a large plankton corpus with a ranked contrastive objective extended to deep, ragged taxonomies, then frozen to condition a parameter-efficient diffusion transformer. We evaluate synthetic sample quality on distributional fidelity and downstream classifier utility.

</details>


### [67] [Single-Stream Multi-Feature Fusion with Temporal Robustness for Gait Emotion Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11680)
*Shirong Lyu,Silu Quan,Yixuan Ding,Chengpeng Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper addresses challenges in 3D skeleton-based gait emotion recognition, including high annotation costs, data scarcity, and poor generalization on heterogeneous data. The proposed SV-GCN framework introduces intra-frame relative motion features and a global mask-guided valid-frame spatio-temporal graph convolution module to achieve frame-rate insensitivity and effective early fusion without multi-stream complexity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to overcome three key challenges in 3D skeleton-based gait emotion recognition: (1) high annotation costs, (2) data scarcity, and (3) poor generalization on heterogeneous data (varying sequence lengths and frame rates). Existing methods struggle with frame-rate sensitivity and require complex multi-stream architectures for fusing heterogeneous cues.

Method: The paper proposes SV-GCN, a single-stream multi-feature fusion framework with temporal invariance. Key innovations include: (1) intra-frame relative motion features to eliminate frame-rate sensitivity, (2) embedding heterogeneous cues at shallow layers for early fusion without multi-stream complexity, and (3) a global mask-guided valid-frame spatio-temporal graph convolution module that handles variable-length sequences while introducing frame-rate insensitivity.

Result: On the E-Gait dataset, SV-GCN achieves performance comparable to state-of-the-art methods while demonstrating strong generalization across varying sequence lengths and frame rates.

Conclusion: The proposed SV-GCN offers a viable pathway for pre-training on large-scale skeleton-based action recognition datasets, potentially addressing data scarcity and annotation cost issues through transfer learning.

Abstract: 3D skeleton-based gait emotion recognition faces high annotation costs, data scarcity, and poor generalization on heterogeneous data. This paper proposes SV-GCN, a single-stream multi-feature fusion framework with temporal invariance. We introduce intra-frame relative motion features to eliminate frame-rate sensitivity and embed heterogeneous cues at shallow layers, enabling early fusion without multi-stream complexity. For variable-length sequences, we design a global mask-guided valid-frame spatio-temporal graph convolution module, introducing frame-rate insensitivity for the first time in this domain. On the E-Gait dataset, our method achieves performance comparable to state-of-the-art while demonstrating strong generalization across varying sequence lengths and frame rates, offering a viable pathway for pre-training on large-scale skeleton-based action recognition datasets.

</details>


### [68] [Spectral Adapters for Segment Anything Model-based Segmentation of Colorectal Liver Metastases in Computed Tomography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11703)
*Ramtin Mojtahedi,Mohammad Hamghalam,Jacob J. Peoples,Natalie Gangai,Mithat Gonen,Yun Shin Chun,HyunSeon Christine Kang,Richard K. G. Do,Amber L. Simpson*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes two parameter-efficient spectral adapters (DiSECT and SiGA) to adapt the Segment Anything Model (SAM) for colorectal liver metastases (CRLM) segmentation in contrast-enhanced CT scans. SiGA achieves 0.77 Dice with single-point prompts and 0.76 Dice without prompts, comparable to nnU-Net baseline, while using only 0.14M trainable parameters (DiSECT).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Accurate CRLM segmentation in contrast-enhanced CT is critical for clinical tasks including response assessment, surgical planning, and follow-up. However, existing methods either lack efficiency or require extensive computational resources. The Segment Anything Model (SAM) shows strong zero-shot capabilities but needs adaptation for medical imaging tasks like CRLM segmentation.

Method: The authors propose two spectral adapter approaches for SAM: (1) DiSECT uses singular value decomposition (SVD) of frozen weights to constrain residual updates to leading spectral directions, reducing trainable parameters; (2) SiGA adds global and input-conditioned gating through a multilayer perceptron (MLP) to enhance adaptation capacity. The methods were evaluated on 446 contrast-enhanced CT volumes (355 training, 91 testing) under four prompt regimes: single-point, three-point, bounding-box, and no-prompt.

Result: SiGA achieves the best single-point performance with Dice score of 0.77, IoU of 0.69, and HD95 of 35.39 mm. Under no-prompt inference, SiGA reaches 0.76 Dice, 0.68 IoU, and 46.76 mm HD95, comparable to the 3D nnU-Net baseline (0.758 Dice). DiSECT uses only 0.14 million trainable parameters, demonstrating extreme parameter efficiency. Both methods outperform or match LoRA, QLoRA, and CAD baselines across most evaluation settings.

Conclusion: Spectral adapters can efficiently adapt SAM for CRLM segmentation while retaining strong accuracy with limited trainable parameters. SiGA achieves state-of-the-art performance with minimal prompts, while DiSECT offers extreme parameter efficiency (0.14M parameters). The approach bridges the gap between large foundation models and practical clinical deployment for medical image segmentation.

Abstract: Accurate segmentation of colorectal liver metastases (CRLM) in contrast-enhanced computed tomography (CT) is important for response assessment, surgical planning, and follow-up. We propose two parameter-efficient spectral adapters for the Segment Anything Model (SAM): the Directional Spectral Adapter (DiSECT) and Spectral Instance-Guided Adapter (SiGA). DiSECT uses singular value decomposition of frozen weights to constrain residual updates to leading spectral directions, while SiGA adds global and input-conditioned gating through a multilayer perceptron. We evaluate these methods on 446 contrast-enhanced CT volumes (355 training, 91 testing) and compare them with LoRA, QLoRA, convolutional adapters (CAD), and a 3D nnU-Net baseline. Experiments consider single-point, three-point, bounding-box, and no-prompt regimes. SiGA achieves the best single-point performance with a Dice score of 0.77, IoU of 0.69, and HD95 of 35.39 mm. Under no-prompt inference, SiGA reaches 0.76 Dice, 0.68 IoU, and 46.76 mm HD95, comparable to the nnU-Net baseline (0.758 Dice). DiSECT uses only 0.14 million trainable parameters. These results show that spectral adapters can efficiently adapt SAM for CRLM segmentation while retaining strong accuracy with limited trainable parameters.

</details>


### [69] [Language-Augmented Semantic Priors for B-Spline Surface Fitting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11708)
*Yunzhong Lou,Yusheng Luo,Jiahao Li,Yu Song,Xiangdong Zhou*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LASP is a framework that uses large language models to infer B-spline prior parameters from procedural modeling histories in CAD systems, bridging the gap between high-level design intent and geometric solver configuration by operating as a semantic reasoning layer above existing solvers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional CAD geometric kernels rely on predetermined heuristic initialization for surface fitting, ignoring procedural semantics and design intent encoded in modeling histories. This disconnect leads to suboptimal and semantically inconsistent fitting results.

Method: LASP translates modeling histories into rich textual descriptions capturing design intent, geometric context, and functional relationships, then uses a fine-tuned LLM to predict structured B-spline prior parameters through a two-stage training scheme combining local geometric regularities with long-range contextual dependencies.

Result: Language-driven reasoning serves as a powerful inductive bias for geometric solving, producing priors that are both interpretable and semantically coherent, directing conventional B-spline fitting toward solutions that more accurately encapsulate design objectives.

Conclusion: LASP establishes a new paradigm of language-guided geometric optimization in modern CAD systems, demonstrating that LLMs can effectively bridge high-level design intent with solver-executable geometric configuration.

Abstract: The use of B-splines and Non-Uniform Rational B-Splines surfaces constitutes the mathematical foundation of contemporary computer-aided design (CAD) systems. Despite long-term progress, geometric kernels in traditional CAD still rely heavily on predetermined heuristic initialization for surface fitting and parameterization. Meanwhile, the procedural semantics and design intent encoded in modeling histories are largely ignored during geometry generation. This disconnect creates a gap between high-level design intent and solver-executable geometric configuration, often leading to suboptimal and semantically inconsistent fitting results. To bridge this gap, we introduce LASP, a Language-Augmented Semantic Priors framework that leverages large language models (LLMs) to infer structured, solver-usable B-spline priors from procedural modeling histories. Rather than modifying the geometric kernel itself, LASP operates as a semantic reasoning layer above existing solvers. It first translates modeling histories into rich textual descriptions that capture design intent, geometric context, and functional relationships, and then uses a fine-tuned LLM to predict structured B-spline prior parameters. LASP is trained through a two-stage scheme that combines local geometric regularities with long-range contextual dependencies, producing priors that are both interpretable and semantically coherent. This approach furnishes inductive signals that direct the conventional B-spline fitting process toward solutions that more accurately encapsulate the intended design objectives and demonstrate heightened semantic coherence. Compared to traditional machine learning schemes, the experiments demonstrate that language-driven reasoning can serve as a powerful inductive bias for geometric solving, establishing a new paradigm of language-guided geometric optimization in modern CAD systems.

</details>


### [70] [MC-DeTra: Motion-Consistent Joint Object Detection and Socially-Aware Trajectory Forecasting in Bird's-Eye-View Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11717)
*Vladislav Diuzhev,Dmitry Yudin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper presents MC-DeTra, a motion-consistency enhanced reimplementation of the DeTra model for unified object detection and trajectory forecasting in autonomous driving. It introduces three auxiliary supervision signals (past motion, social context occupancy, and heading-motion consistency) that are used only during training to shape the shared BEV representation, adding no inference latency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Unified detection and forecasting models remain challenging for dynamic, moving actors. The strongest model (DeTra) has no public implementation, limiting research progress. Additionally, existing methods lack proper supervision for motion consistency between predicted headings and actual motion directions.

Method: The authors reimplemented DeTra and added three motion-consistency mechanisms: (1) past motion supervision from observed actor trajectories, (2) social context supervision from surrounding traffic occupancy, and (3) heading-motion consistency loss aligning predicted heading with motion direction. All losses are train-only, shaping the shared BEV representation during training but removed at test time.

Result: On Waymo Open Dataset under strict detection-conditioned forecasting protocol, MC-DeTra improves dynamic, socially-situated trajectory forecasting while preserving or improving detection accuracy. Gradient-based loss-calibration analysis reveals competition between auxiliary objectives at the shared backbone, and ablation identifies the most contributory signals.

Conclusion: The motion-consistency mechanisms successfully improve forecasting accuracy without adding inference latency. The approach demonstrates that train-only auxiliary supervision can effectively shape learned representations. The reimplementation and evaluation tooling are publicly released to advance research in this area.

Abstract: Unified models for object detection and trajectory forecasting aim to merge perception and prediction for autonomous driving, refining actor trajectories directly over shared bird's-eye-view (BEV) images rasterized from LiDAR and high-definition maps. Their accuracy on dynamic, moving actors, however, remains the hardest part of the task, and the strongest such model, DeTra, has no public implementation. We contribute an openly released DeTra reimplementation with documented approximations, and on top of it MC-DeTra: a family of motion-consistency mechanisms that add supervision through two annotation-derived auxiliary signals -- each actor's observed past motion and the occupancy of the surrounding traffic that forms its social context -- and one inter-output consistency constraint that aligns an actor's predicted heading with its predicted direction of motion. Every proposed loss is train-only and inference-safe: it shapes the shared BEV representation during training and is removed at test time, adding no inference latency. On the Waymo Open Dataset, evaluated under a strict, detection-conditioned forecasting protocol, MC-DeTra improves dynamic, socially-situated trajectory forecasting while preserving or improving detection accuracy; a gradient-based loss-calibration analysis exposes how the auxiliary objectives compete at the shared backbone, and our ablation identifies which signals contribute most. We release code, configurations, and evaluation tooling at https://github.com/diuzhevVlad/MC-DeTra.

</details>


### [71] [Revisiting Avatar-As-Image: High-Fidelity Registration is All You Need](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11722)
*Margaret Kostyrko,Yuxuan Xue,Garvita Tiwari,Gerard Pons-Moll*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AvaImg is a multi-stage optimization pipeline that creates high-fidelity SMPL(-X)+D registrations with UV texture and displacement maps from arbitrary 3D clothed human scans, enabling use of pretrained image networks for 3D avatar processing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior UV-based methods for clothed humans have limited quality because scans were never accurately aligned and brought into correspondence via high-fidelity registration. No public method existed to produce high-quality SMPL(-X)+D registrations with UV texture from arbitrary clothed scans.

Method: AvaImg uses a multi-stage optimization pipeline with: (1) body-inside-clothing constraint via signed winding numbers, made viable by a three-level efficiency cascade (~10x runtime reduced, ~95% storage saved), and (2) coarse-to-fine displacement optimization to recover fine surface detail.

Result: AvaImg outperforms all baselines in body fitting, shape estimation, and surface registration across six datasets, achieving PSNR=34.48dB (near-indistinguishable from scans). When validating Avatar-as-Image representation with FLUX VAE auto-encoding, only 0.76mm added Chamfer error was introduced, and the maps lie within natural-image distributions.

Conclusion: The UV-based avatar representation is compatible with 2D image foundation models, supporting the use of 2D generative priors for 3D avatar generation. The method enables pretrained image networks to process, generate, and edit 3D avatars through standardized 2D UV texture and displacement maps.

Abstract: The representation of 3D clothed humans as standardized 2D UV texture and displacement maps over an underlying body model has long been studied. This compact representation is enticing as it enables pretrained image networks to process, generate, and edit 3D avatars, but is only useful if scans are accurately aligned and brought into correspondence via high-fidelity registration. This prerequisite has never been met, which we argue explains the limited quality of prior UV-based methods for clothed humans. Despite its significance, no public method produces high-fidelity SMPL(-X)+D registrations with UV texture from arbitrary clothed scans. We present AvaImg, a multi-stage optimization pipeline, to close this gap: it enforces body-inside-clothing constraint via signed winding numbers, made viable by a three-level efficiency cascade (~10x runtime reduced, ~95% storage saved), and recovers fine surface detail using coarse-to-fine displacement optimization. AvaImg outperforms all baselines in body fitting, shape estimation, and surface registration across six datasets, yielding textured registrations near-indistinguishable from scans (PSNR=34.48dB).
